Figure 7.
Translation from
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
into
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{223985}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{223985}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{156789}{124436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{111992}{88883}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
. Variable names introduced on the right-hand side are always fresh.

The penultimate step towards deriving the graph IR is restricting the
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
language to

monadic normal form
(MNF), called
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
(
Figure

6
). We establish soundness of

\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
by (1) showing that
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
’s reduction relation with store-allocated values (
\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathbf{sv}}}
,

Figure

5
) preserves MNF, (2) specifying provably type-preserving
translations between both languages, so that (3) we can resort to the previous section’s soundness
result for
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
.

4.1.
Syntax

We make use of the syntactic category of names in places where both variables and locations
are permitted, written in typewriter font.

MNF (
Figure

6
) is characterized by having all
intermediate results and subterms of expressions let-bound to variable names. Unlike A-normal form
(ANF), which has strictly flat sequences of let bindings with primitive operations, MNF permits
binding nested computations. A (directed, acyclic) graph can be read from graph terms
g
, by
regarding let bindings as introducing a name for either (1) a primitive graph node labelled with a
primitive operation drawn from
n
, or (2) naming a nested subgraph
g
. Variable
occurrences in bound nodes correspond to edges pointing to the let binding in scope.

In this work, we choose an even stricter form of MNF than usual,
i.e.
, sequences of let bindings
in graph terms
g
always end with a name. We found that this more regular form is
easier to work with when specifying optimization rules.

4.2.
Reduction Preserves MNF

The reduction relation for
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
with store-allocated values (
Figure

5
) preserves MNF, and
can thus be restricted to obtain the call-by-value reduction relation for
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
:

Lemma 4.1 (Reduction Preserves MNF).

Let
g
be a graph term in
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
, and
\sigma
a store that only binds
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
introductions, such that
\sigma\mid g\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathbf{sv}}}\sigma^{\prime}\mid t

for some
\sigma^{\prime}
and term
t
. Then it holds that

(1)

\sigma^{\prime}
is a store binding only
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
introductions.

(2)

t
is a graph term of
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
.

Proof.

Since the reduction step begins with a graph term
g
, it can be only decomposed into a redex and evaluation context
according to
E::=\square\mid\textsf{{let}}\ {x}={E}\ \textsf{{in}}\ {g}
, and no other cases apply. Redexes in the hole can only be bindings
b
,
i.e.
,
either a node
n
or a nested graph term
g^{\prime}
. It is easy to see that each possible reduction rule focuses on such a binding,
and each rule plugs the hole with another binding
b^{\prime}
on the right-hand side, thus preserving MNF.
Furthermore,
\sigma^{\prime}
is either equal to
\sigma
, or a modification of the latter where each binding is in MNF.
∎

4.3.
Translation from Direct Style to MNF

This section considers the syntax-directed translation of
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
into
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
(
Figure

7
).

Lemma 4.2 (Type Preservation of the MNF Translation).

If
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
and
t\leadsto g
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.

Proof.

Straightforward by induction over the typing derivation
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.
We exemplify the proof for applications (
t-app
). In this case

t_{1}~t_{2}\leadsto\text{{{let}}}~{x_{1}=g_{1}}~\text{{{in}}}~\text{{{let}}}~{x_{2}=g_{2}}~\text{{{in}}}~\text{{{let}}}~{x_{3}=x_{1}~x_{2}}~\text{{{in}}}~x_{3}

where
t_{1}\leadsto g_{1}
and
t_{2}\leadsto g_{2}
.

(1)

We have
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\vdash t_{1}:\left(x{\,:\,}T^{\,p\mathord{*}\,{\cap}\,q\mathord{*}}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}}}U^{\,r}\right)^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
.

(2)

We have
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{2}:T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
.

(3)

We have
x\notin\operatorname{fv}(U)
,
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}\subseteq q,x
,
r\subseteq\varphi,x
, and
\theta=[p/x]
.

(4)

By IH:
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,g_{1}:\left(x{\,:\,}T^{\,p\mathord{*}\,{\cap}\,q\mathord{*}}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}}}U^{\,r}\right)^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
.

(5)

By IH:
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,g_{2}:T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
.

(6)

By weakening:
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma,x_{1}:\left(x{\,:\,}T^{\,p\mathord{*}\,{\cap}\,q\mathord{*}}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}}}U^{\,r}\right)^{\,q}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi,x_{1}}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,g_{2}:T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
.

(7)

Let
\Gamma^{\prime}:=\Gamma,x_{1}:\left(x{\,:\,}T^{\,p\,{\cap}\,q}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}}}U^{\,r}\right)^{\,q},x_{2}:T^{\,p}
.

(8)

By rule (
n-app
) and (3):
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi,x_{1},x_{2}}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,x_{1}~x_{2}:U^{\,r\theta}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}\theta
.

(9)

By (
g-let
) and (
g-ret
):
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi,x_{1},x_{2}}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,\text{{{let}}}~{x_{3}=x_{1}~x_{2}}~\text{{{in}}}~x_{3}:U^{\,r\theta}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}\theta
.

(10)

With (6) and (
g-let
):

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma,x_{1}:\left(x{\,:\,}T^{\,p\mathord{*}\,{\cap}\,q\mathord{*}}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}}}U^{\,r}\right)^{\,q}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi,x_{1}}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,\text{{{let}}}~{x_{2}=g_{2}}~\text{{{in}}}~\text{{{let}}}~{x_{3}=x_{1}~x_{2}}~\text{{{in}}}~x_{3}:(U^{\,r\theta}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}\theta)[p/x_{2}]

(11)

With (4) and (
g-let
):

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,\text{{{let}}}~{x_{1}=g_{1}}~\text{{{in}}}~\text{{{let}}}~{x_{2}=g_{2}}~\text{{{in}}}~\text{{{let}}}~{x_{3}=x_{1}~x_{2}}~\text{{{in}}}~x_{3}:(U^{\,r\theta{[p/x_{2}]}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}\theta)[p/x_{2}])[q/x_{1}]

(12)

Since
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
were picked fresh, and
x
is not free in
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
by (1) and (2), we have

(U^{\,r\theta{[p/x_{2}]}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}\theta)[p/x_{2}])[q/x_{1}]=U^{\,r\theta}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}\theta=(U^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}})\theta.

That is, (11) proves the goal.

∎

4.4.
Soundness

Instead of proving progress and preservation directly, we assert that terms in monadic normal form
can always be typed in the same manner in both the direct style and MNF type systems.
The intention is that we have the same type system, but restricted in the terms.

Lemma 4.3 (Type-preserving Embedding of MNF Terms).

(1)

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,n:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,n:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.

(2)

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.

Proof.

Each direction is proved by mutual induction over the respective typing derivation.
∎

Together with
Lemma

4.1
, it follows
that the type soundness and preservation of separation results of the direct style system
(
Section

3.3
) carry over to the MNF version.

Corollary 4.4 (MNF Progress).

If
\ {\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, then either
g
is a value, or
for any store
\sigma
where
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash\,\sigma
, there exists
a graph term
g^{\prime}
and store
\sigma^{\prime}
such that
\sigma\mid g\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathbf{sv}}}\sigma^{\prime}\mid g^{\prime}
.

Corollary 4.5 (MNF Preservation).

\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash\,\sigma\qquad\sigma\mid g\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathbf{sv}}}\sigma^{\prime}\mid g^{\prime}\end{array}}{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists\Sigma^{\prime}\supseteq\Sigma.\;\exists p\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime}\setminus\Sigma).\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime})}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,g^{\prime}:T^{\,q,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}},p}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime})}\,\vdash\,\sigma^{\prime}\end{array}}

Corollary 4.6 (MNF Preservation of Separation).

Interleaved executions preserve types and disjointness:

\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\begin{array}[]{l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,g_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}&\sigma\phantom{{}^{\prime}}\mid g_{1}\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathbf{sv}}}\sigma^{\prime}\phantom{{}^{\prime}}\mid g_{1}^{\prime}&{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash\,\sigma&\\[4.30554pt]

\sigma\mid z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{{{let}}}~{x=\iota\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}~\text{{{in}}}~g}\,]}&\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{170654}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{170654}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{119458}{124436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{85327}{88883}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}}&\sigma,\text{{{let}}}_{\mathsf{s}}~{\ell=\iota}\mid z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G^{\prime}[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}]}[\ell/x][\ell/x]_{\mathsf{t}}}\,]}&&(\textsc{intro})\\
&&\ell\not\in\operatorname{dom}(\sigma),\ \operatorname{cod}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})\subseteq\{z\},\ G^{\prime}=G\langle\iota:z.\ell\rangle&&\\[3.87498pt]
\sigma,\,\text{{{let}}}_{\mathsf{s}}~{\ell=\text{{{ref}}}_{w}~\ell^{\prime}},\,\sigma^{\prime}\mid z.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}B[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}!\ell\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}\,]}&\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{170654}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{170654}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{119458}{124436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{85327}{88883}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}}&\sigma,\,\text{{{let}}}_{\mathsf{s}}~{\ell=\text{{{ref}}}_{w}~\ell^{\prime}},\,\sigma^{\prime}\mid z.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}B[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ell^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}\,]}&&(\textsc{deref})\\
&&\operatorname{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\sigma),\ \operatorname{cod}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})\subseteq\{z\}&&\\[3.87498pt]
\sigma,\,\text{{{let}}}_{\mathsf{s}}~{\ell=\text{{{ref}}}_{w}~\ell^{\prime}},\,\sigma^{\prime}\mid z.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}B[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ell:=\ell^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}\,]}&\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{170654}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{170654}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{119458}{124436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{85327}{88883}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}}&\sigma,\,\text{{{let}}}_{\mathsf{s}}~{\ell=\text{{{ref}}}_{w}~\ell^{\prime\prime}},\,\sigma^{\prime}\mid z.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}B[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{unit}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}\,]}&&(\textsc{assign})\\
&&\operatorname{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\sigma),\ \operatorname{cod}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})\subseteq\{z\}&&\end{array}

Contextual Effect Propagation

G\langle\iota:z.\ell\rangle

\begin{array}[]{l@{\ \ }c@{\ \ }l}G\langle c:z.\ell\rangle&=&G\\
G\langle\lambda x.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}:z.\ell\rangle&=&G\\
(\square\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})\langle\text{{{ref}}}_{w}~\ell_{1}:z.\ell_{2}\rangle&=&\square\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta},\ell_{2}\mapsto z}\\
((\text{{{let}}}~{x=G}~\text{{{in}}}~g)\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})\langle\text{{{ref}}}_{w}~\ell_{1}:z.\ell_{2}\rangle&=&(\text{{{let}}}~{x=G\langle\text{{{ref}}}_{w}~\ell_{1}:z.\ell_{2}\rangle}~\text{{{in}}}~g)\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta},\ell_{2}\mapsto z}\\

Well-Formed Store Entries and Stores

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\,\vdash\,\ell:\iota\in\sigma

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\,\vdash\,\sigma

Figure 10.
Call-by-value reduction for
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{170654}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{170654}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{119458}{124436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{85327}{88883}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}
with runtime dependency checking.

This section presents our graph IR
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}
which enriches the monadic normal form (MNF) of the previous section
with effect dependencies.

5.1.
Dependencies

Data dependencies are expressed by ordinary variable occurrences in terms. Tracking effect dependencies requires
extra term annotations.
Intuitively, a (hard) effect dependency
x\mapsto y
indicates that an effect
on the node
x
(
e.g.
, a reference, or global capability) is induced, and that node
y
is the previous
node in the graph at which an effect on
x
occurred. That is to say,
x\mapsto y

does not

indicate there is an edge between
x
and
y
, but rather that there is an edge
from the current node

to which
x\mapsto y
is attached pointing to
y
and its (effect) label is
x
.
Oftentimes we will just say “dependency” and omit the “hard” and “effect” qualifiers.

We bundle these dependencies into finite maps
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
from variables to variables, and annotate them
to atomic nodes or nested graphs at let bindings.
Dependencies come with a standard "update" operator which is associative:

({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}})(x):=\begin{cases}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}(x)&x\in\operatorname{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}})\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}(x)&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

and a restriction operator of the domain to a given set (abusing set notation):

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}|_{\alpha}:=\{x\mapsto y\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\mid x\in\alpha\},

and we also define two removal operators, i.e.,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}-\alpha:=\{x\mapsto y\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\mid y\notin\alpha\},

which removes all mappings pointing into
\alpha
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\setminus\alpha:={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}|_{\operatorname{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})\setminus\alpha},

which removes all entries pointing from
\alpha
.
In symmetry with substitutions on terms and qualifiers, there is a notion of substitution (or rewiring, rerouting), over dependencies:

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}]:={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}-\{x\},\{y\mapsto z\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}\mid y\mapsto x\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}\},

which is rerouting the dependency target
x
via
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}
. For instance,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}(a\mapsto b,c\mapsto x,y\mapsto x)[x\leadsto(c\mapsto a,y\mapsto b)]}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}(a\mapsto b,c\mapsto a,y\mapsto b)}.

That is, rewiring substitutes some of the targets in dependency mappings, which happens when the variable
x
is replaced, e.g.,
when
x
is the formal parameter of a function at a call site. The function body may have dependencies pointing to
x
, and these
must be rerouted using the call-site’s dependencies. Dually,
x
might also be in the domain of a dependency, and the monadic
semantics will rename it to some store location holding the value of the call site argument. For this case, we also
lift qualifier substitutions to act on the domain of dependencies, i.e.,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}{[q/x]}:={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\setminus x,\{y\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}(x)\mid y\in q\}

so that all variables and locations in
q
will point to
x
’s target, if it has an entry.

Effect-dependency calculation

Static effects
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
and contextual information on last uses determine effect dependencies
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
at graph nodes.
We outline the basic process in the following.

A few auxiliary definitions are in order:

\displaystyle\alpha\mapsto x

\displaystyle:=\{y\mapsto x\mid y\in\alpha\}

\displaystyle\Sigma,\Gamma\mapsto x

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)\cup\operatorname{dom}(\Gamma)\mapsto x

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\Sigma\mid\Gamma\vdash\,}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}x

\displaystyle:=\Sigma,\Gamma\mapsto x,\ \Sigma,\Gamma\ \text{are implicit}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\Sigma\mid\Gamma\vdash\,}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\uparrow^{z}

\displaystyle:={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta},\{x\mapsto z\mid x\in(\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)\cup\operatorname{dom}(\Gamma))\setminus\operatorname{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})\},\ \Sigma,\Gamma\ \text{are implicit}

Firstly, we overload the finite maps notation to specify a map with domain
\alpha
pointing to
the single variable
x
, and analogously the map pointing all the variables and locations bound in

\Gamma
and
\Sigma
to
x
, for which we often use the shorthand notation
\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}x

omitting those contexts when they are unambiguous. The
\uparrow^{z}
operator is used to ensure
that a dependency is defined for at least the variables and location in context, adding entries
pointing to a common block start variable
z
.

If a nested graph
named
x
has effect
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, then we record that the reachable variables affected by
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathord{*}}
5
5

5

Since the systems in this report
lazily
assign qualifiers and effects, we need to consider the transitive reachability closure
(
Figure

3
) to get ahold of all relevant dependencies.

were last used at
x
(plus
x
was last used at itself, explained below). So if

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}
records the last use of each variable in scope (essentially a structural coeffect
which is also just a finite map from variables to variables), then we update it to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta,({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathord{*}},x\mapsto x)}
. Synthesizing the dependency for node
x
itself is just a
matter of projecting the last-use coeffect
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}
.

Concretely, consider
\text{{{let}}}~{x=g_{1}}~\text{{{in}}}~g_{2}
with last uses
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}
, and let
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}

be the effect of
g_{1}
. We first calculate the annotated version of
g_{1}
:

g_{1}\leadsto g_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathord{*}}}}}

which attaches the current last uses with
respect to
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
. When proceeding with the continuation
g_{2}
,
x
is added into the context,
and we need to define its last-use coeffect, which is simply
x
itself.
We will discuss the precise calculation rules of our type system in the next section.

5.2.
Syntax and Statics

Figure

8
shows the type checking relation,
and it is easy too see that the
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}
-calculus is identical to the
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
-calculus (
Figure

6
),
when erasing all the
teal
parts pertaining to dependencies. At the term level, we attach
dependencies
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
to let bindings (representing the dependencies for all the effects of the bound graph term) and
the body of
\lambda
-abstractions (representing the dependencies of the abstraction’s latent effects)

5.2.1.
Type Checking

The typing judgment now carries an additional dependency map
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}
attached to the context
(basically a form of coeffect), which is used to track the
last uses
of variables and
locations in the context/store. We stipulate that at all times the domain of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}
ranges over
the domain of
\Gamma
and
\Sigma
. Last uses are threaded as an input through typing derivations,
and the only rules at which they are accessed are those for terms with dependency annotations,
i.e.
,
(
n-abs
) and (
g-let
). Those annotated dependencies should always conform to the
effect of the term in question, and as a rule of thumb, we regard the effect
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
’s transitive
reachability closure (a set of variables and locations) as a slice of the currently known last uses

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}
,
i.e.
, restricting the domain of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}
to the effect in question yields all the
relevant dependencies at the current node.

In the rule (
n-abs
) for
\lambda
-abstractions, we check the body with all the last uses
pointing to the formal parameter
x
. This is because we generally do not know the call site and
actual argument in advance, and the most natural choice is abstracting the last use of the free variables in
the body by
x
in symmetry to term abstraction. As we have motivated in the main paper and will shortly see
in the operational semantics (
Section

5.3
), the “latent” dependency
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
annotated to the
function’s body has to be rewired at the call site.
Following the “rule of thumb” above, we check that the annotated
dependency
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
is conforming to the body’s dependency in relation to its last uses,
i.e.
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
is
a sub-map of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathord{*}}\mapsto x}
.

Similarly, rule (
g-let
) checks that the annotated dependency for the bound node/nested
graph is conforming to its effect,
i.e.
, it is a sub-map of the last uses for
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}

in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}
. When typing the let body
g
, we update the last uses for the variables affected by

{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}
and let them point to the binding
x
, precisely because those variables
have been last used here. Furthermore, since
x
is newly introduced in the body
g
, we also
have to specify a last use for it, which is
x
itself.

5.2.2.
Dependency Synthesis

While the typing relation provides a means to check dependencies, it does not provide a method to
compute them. For this purpose we define a type/qualifier/effect-directed synthesis relation
(
Figure

9
) for
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
terms which lack any dependency annotations and produces
dependency-annotated
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}
terms along with the dependency map for effects on free variables as
output, given an initial map
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}
of last uses and typing context as input.
Synthesizing the dependencies follows the “rule of thumb” from above,
i.e.
, synthesized and inserted
dependencies are always the currently known last uses of the variables in the term’s effect
(cf.
Section

5.4.1
).

5.3.
Dynamics

The call-by-value operational semantics for
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}
(
Figure

10
) is a refinement of the operational semantics for
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
with
store-allocated values (
Figure

5
). The changes are twofold: (1)
dependencies are part of the term syntax now, and have to be accounted for by reductions, and (2)
the semantics additionally checks for each reduction step whether all the effect dependencies have
been already evaluated and committed to the store. By type soundness
(cf.
Section

5.4
), well-typed terms do not exhibit dependency violations,
i.e.
,
dependencies correctly reflect the observed runtime execution order of effect operations.

Reduction occurs over runtime configurations
\sigma\mid z.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
, which compared to the

\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
-calculus attaches a distinguished
start variable

z
to the graph term
g
along
with its dependency
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
. We stipulate that
z
is always chosen so that it is not a free
variable of
g
. It is a mechanism to check whether a dependency has already been evaluated. That
is, at the top level, we set the initial use of all the free variables/locations to
z
, and that
will be reflected in the synthesized dependencies of a term. The invariant is that the next
operation will have all its dependencies purely on store variables and these will point to
z
,
which is outside of the program. The meaning is that all dependencies of the current node are in the
store, and each of the reduction rules checks this property. In the following, we discuss the
changes made to the reduction rules compared to
Figure

5
.

In the function application rule (
\beta
), we now have to account for the latent
dependencies of the function and the dependencies at the call site. Thus, in symmetry with dependent
function application, we rewire the function body and its dependency
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}

for
x
. Since dependencies are type-level information annotated in the term syntax, we also have
to perform the qualifier substitution part of the static dependent function application,
i.e.
, we
substitute the argument’s location
\ell_{2}
for
x
in the domain of dependencies, if present.
To distinguish qualifier substitution in dependencies from term substitution/renaming of variables,
we attach the subscript
\mathsf{t}
to the latter. Hence, substitution becomes simultaneous
rewiring, qualifier substitution, and renaming on terms in the graph IR.
Accordingly, we do the same in (
let
) and (
intro
).

Rule (
intro
) has changed compared to
Figure

5
in that it
simultaneously performs the (
let
) elimination along with the introduction of the fresh
location. The reason is that from the perspective of static typing,
introducing the new location will make it appear in reachability qualifiers and effects, and
consequently in dependencies. So the reduction step has to patch up the dependencies in the
body of the let expression, because
x
now aliases
\ell
, which it did not beforehand.
Furthermore, if
\iota
is a mutable reference, using it makes it also appear in the codomain of
effects dependencies, and this change needs propagating into all dependencies along the spine of
the evaluation context up to the top level, because
\ell
is globally visible.

Finally, modulo checking effect dependencies, rules (
deref
) and (
assign
) have not
changed.

5.4.
Metatheory

5.4.1.
Properties of Dependency Synthesis

Dependency synthesis induces a function over MNF typing derivations which given an input map of last
uses always produce an annotated graph IR term with the same type, qualifier, and effect. As a
corollary, we obtain a type/effect/qualifier-preserving and dependency-synthesizing translation from
the direct style
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
system into the
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}
graph IR.

Lemma 5.1 (Synthesis Invariant).

Dependencies are completely determined by the context and effect, as follows:

(1)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\vdash n:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\ \leadsto\bm{n}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}},
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathord{*}}}
.

(2)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\vdash g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\ \leadsto\bm{g}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}},
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathord{*}}}
.

Proof.

By mutual induction over the respective derivation. Most cases are straightforward. The case
for rule
(
\leadsto
-let)
requires careful reasoning about dependency maps:

•

Case (
\leadsto
-let
): We need to show
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}]}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\theta)\mathord{*}}}
for
\theta=[p/x]
.

(1)

By IH:
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}}
.

(2)

By IH:
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}},x)\mapsto x})|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}}
.

(3)

By scoping:
x\notin\operatorname{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta})
, and
x\notin\operatorname{cod}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta})
.

(4)

Case distinction:

(a)

x\notin{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
: Thus,

\begin{array}[]{rcll}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}]}&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}&\text{by}\ x\notin{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}},({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}},x)\mapsto x})|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}}&\text{by (1), (2)}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}}&\text{by}\ x\notin{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\ \text{and def. of}\ \_,\_\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}},{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}}&\text{by definition of}\ \_|_{\_}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}}&\text{by definition of}\ \_\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}\_\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})\mathord{*}}}&\text{by properties of}\ \_\mathord{*}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\theta)\mathord{*}}}\quad\checkmark&\text{by}\ x\notin{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\\
\end{array}

(b)

x\in{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}:

(i)

We have that

\begin{array}[]{rcll}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}&=&({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}},x)\mapsto x})|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}}&\text{by (2)}\\
&=&({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}},x)\mapsto x})|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}}&\text{by def. of}\ \_,\_\\
&=&({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}})|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}\mapsto x)|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}},(x\mapsto x)|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}}}&\text{by prop. of}\ \_|_{\_}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}\mapsto x)|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}},(x\mapsto x)|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}}}&\text{by prop. of}\ \_|_{\_}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}\mapsto x),x\mapsto x}&\text{by }x\in{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}},\ \text{and prop. of}\ \_|_{\_}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}},x},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}\mapsto x),x\mapsto x}&\text{by }x\notin\operatorname{cod}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}}\\
\end{array}

(ii)

From
x\in{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
and properties of reachability saturation, we have that
p\mathord{*}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}
.

(iii)

From that, and
x\in{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
, and properties of saturation, we have either
p\mathord{*}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}
or

p\mathord{*}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}=\varnothing
. Case distinction:

(iv)

Case
p\mathord{*}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}
: Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}=p\mathord{*},q
where
p\mathord{*}\cap q=\varnothing

(A)

That with (4).(b).(i) yields
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},x},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}(p\mathord{*},q\mapsto x),x\mapsto x}

(B)

Thus,

\begin{array}[]{rcll}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}]}&=&({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},x},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}(p\mathord{*},q\mapsto x),x\mapsto x}){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]}&\text{by (A)}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},x}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}(p\mathord{*},q\mapsto x){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]},x\mapsto x{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]}}&\text{by prop. of }\leadsto\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},x}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}(p\mathord{*},q\mapsto x){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]}}&\text{by }x\notin\operatorname{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}})\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},x}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}(p\mathord{*}\mapsto x){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]}}&\text{by (iv)}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},x}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}}&\text{by def. of }\leadsto\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},x},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}}&\text{by }x\notin\operatorname{cod}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta})\\
\end{array}

(C)

From that, and (1), we conclude

\begin{array}[]{rcll}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]}&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},x},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}}&\text{}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus x,{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}p\mathord{*}}}&\text{by definition of}\ \_|_{\_}\text{ and prop. of sets}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}[p\mathord{*}/x]}&\text{by def. of substitution and effect composition}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\theta)\mathord{*}}}\quad\checkmark&\text{by prop. of saturation}\\
\end{array}

(v)

Case
p\mathord{*}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}=\varnothing
: Thus with (ii), we have
p\mathord{*}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}
.

(A)

Thus,

\begin{array}[]{rcll}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]}&=&({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},x},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\mapsto x),x\mapsto x}){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]}&\text{by (i)}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},x}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]}&\text{by (v) and }x\notin\operatorname{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}})\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},x}&\text{by }x\notin\operatorname{cod}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta})\end{array}

(B)

From that, and (1), we conclude

\begin{array}[]{rcll}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{p}\mathord{*}]}&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},x}&\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}\setminus x,{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}p\mathord{*}}}&\text{by (v)}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathord{*}}[p\mathord{*}/x]}&\text{by def. of substitution and effect composition}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\theta)\mathord{*}}}\quad\checkmark&\text{by prop. of saturation}\\
\end{array}

∎

The above proof of
Lemma

5.1
, justifies that we could alternatively pick the dependency
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\theta)\mathord{*}}}

as the synthesis result in the conclusion of rule (
\leadsto
-let
), and certifies that sequential dependency map composition and
rewiring are consistent with effect composition and substitution, provided that the effects are saturated in the context.

Lemma 5.2 (Soundness of Synthesis).

Synthesis produces well-typed annotated programs:

(1)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\,\vdash\,n:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\ \leadsto\bm{n}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}},
then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\vdash\bm{n}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.

(2)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\,\vdash\,g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\ \leadsto\bm{g}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}},
then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\vdash\bm{g}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.

Proof.

By straightforward mutual induction over the respective derivation, making use of
Lemma

5.1
where appropriate.
∎

Lemma 5.3 (Synthesis is Total).

(1)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,n:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
then
\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}.\ \exists{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\bm{n}}.\ \exists{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}.

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\vdash n:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\ \leadsto\bm{n}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\bm{n}}
erases back to
n
.

(2)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
then
\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}.\ \exists{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\bm{g}}.\ \exists{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}.

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\vdash g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\ \leadsto\bm{g}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\bm{g}}
erases back to
g
.

Proof.

By straightforward mutual induction over the respective derivations, exploiting that typing rules of each system are in one-to-one correspondence.
∎

Corollary 5.4 (End-to-end Type Preservation).

For any well-formed context and compatible
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}
, there is a type/effect/qualifier-preserving translation from the direct-style
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
-calculus into
the
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}
graph IR.

Proof.

By lemmas
4.2
,
5.3
, and
5.2
.
∎

5.4.2.
Substitution and Rewiring

Lemma 5.5 (Top-Level Rewiring and Substitution).

If
\ \Sigma\mid\Gamma,x:S^{\,p\cap r}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}.z\ \mathsf{ok}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,p}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\varnothing}\,\vdash\,\ell:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}
, and
\theta=[p/x]

where
p\subseteq\mbox{\sl dom}(\Sigma)
and
p\cap\varphi\subseteq p\cap r
, and
\operatorname{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)
, and
\operatorname{cod}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})\subseteq\{z\}
then

\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma,x:S^{\,p\cap r}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\,\vdash\,n:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\end{array}}{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma\theta{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi\theta}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\uparrow^{z}]\theta}\,\vdash\,n{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}]}\theta[\ell/x]_{\mathsf{t}}:(T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}})\theta\end{array}}

(1)

\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma,x:S^{\,p\cap r}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\,\vdash\,g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\end{array}}{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma\theta{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi\theta}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\uparrow^{z}]\theta}\,\vdash\,g{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}]}\theta[\ell/x]_{\mathsf{t}}:(T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}})\theta\end{array}}

(2)

Proof.

The proof proceeds by mutual induction over the respective derivation. Ignoring dependencies, each case uses similar
reasoning steps as the previous substitution lemma proof for the system with store-allocated values (
Lemma

3.1
).
We focus here only on the interesting cases involving dependencies, which are the typing rules for
\lambda
-abstraction and let bindings.

•

Case (
n-abs
): That is,
n=\lambda y.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}
.

(1)

We have
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma\,,\,x:S^{\,p\cap r}\,,\,y:T^{\,p^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,q,y}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}y}\,\vdash\,g:U^{\,r^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}
.

(2)

We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\mathord{*}}\mapsto y}
.

(3)

By IH:
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma\theta,\,y:T\theta^{\,p^{\prime}\theta}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,q\theta,y}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}y[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\uparrow^{z}]\theta)}\,\vdash\,g{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}]}\theta[\ell/x]_{\mathsf{t}}:(U^{\,r^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}})\theta
.

(4)

Since
x\neq y
, it holds that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}y[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\uparrow^{z}]\theta)=\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}y}
.

(5)

By (2), no entry in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}
points to
x
, because it is a submap of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\mathord{*}}\mapsto y}
and
y\neq x
.

(6)

Thus
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}]}\theta={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}\theta
, and by (2) and monotonicity of substitution,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}\theta\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}\theta\mapsto y}
.

(7)

Hence
n{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}]}\theta[\ell/x]_{\mathsf{t}}=\lambda y.(g{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}]}\theta[\ell/x]_{\mathsf{t}})\mathbin{\bullet}\delta^{\prime}\theta
.

(8)

By (3),(6),(7), and (
n-abs
) the proof goal follows.

•

Case (
g-let
): That is,
g=\text{{{let}}}~{y=b\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}}~\text{{{in}}}~g^{\prime}
.

(1)

We have
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma\,,\,x:S^{\,p\cap r}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\,\vdash\,b:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
.

(2)

We have
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma\,,\,x:S^{\,p\cap r}\,,\,y:T^{\,q}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi,y}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},y)\mapsto y}\,\vdash\,g^{\prime}:U^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
.

(3)

We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}}}
.

(4)

By IH:
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma\theta{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi\theta}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\uparrow^{z}]\theta}\,\vdash\,b{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}]}\theta[\ell/x]_{\mathsf{t}}:(T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})\theta
.

(5)

By IH:
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma\theta\,,y:T\theta^{\,q\theta}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi\theta,y}\mathbin{\bullet}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},y)\mapsto y}){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\uparrow^{z}]\theta}\,\vdash\,g^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}]}\theta[\ell/x]_{\mathsf{t}}:(U^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})\theta
.

(6)

From
x\neq y
and the properties of rewiring and qualifier substitution on dependencies, it follows that

({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}},y)\mapsto y}){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\uparrow^{z}]\theta}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\uparrow^{z}]\theta},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}\theta,y)\mapsto y}.

(7)

From (3) and monotonicity of substitution, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}\theta\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\theta|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}\theta}
.

(8)

By (4), (5), (6), (7), and (
g-let
) the proof goal follows.

∎

5.4.3.
Context Typing and Effect Introductions

We have generative effect introductions that modify the runtime context, and thus need lemmas for
plugging/decomposition and growing the stack of dependencies by fresh effect dependencies from
generative effects (i.e., reference allocations in this system).

Definition 5.6 (Context Typings).

We define the typings of graph and binding contexts (
Figure

10
) relative to an ambient block start variable
z
, as follows:

(1)

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot}\,]}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\Rightarrow T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma,x:S^{\,p}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta},x\mapsto z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}\,]}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}=\operatorname{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})
for
the dependency
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
at
G
’s hole.

(2)

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot}\,]}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\Rightarrow T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma,x:S^{\,p}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta},x\mapsto z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}\,]}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}

for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}}
.

Lemma 5.7 (Contextual Effect Propagation).

\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot}\,]}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\Rightarrow T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\quad\ell\notin\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)\end{array}}{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma,\ell:U^{\,r}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G\langle\iota:z.\ell\rangle[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot}\,]}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\ell}\Rightarrow T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\ell}\end{array}}

Proof.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot}\,]}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\Rightarrow T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
,
and
\ell\notin\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)
. We proceed by induction over
G
:

•

Case
G=\square\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
: Hence
G\langle\iota:z.\ell\rangle=\square\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta},\ell\mapsto z}
, and
\Sigma\mid\Gamma\,\vdash\,S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}<:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}=\operatorname{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})
. By the properties of subtyping, it holds that
\Sigma,\ell:U^{\,r}\mid\varnothing\,\vdash\,S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\ell}<:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\ell}
.
Using (
g-ret
) and (
b-sub
) proves the goal.

•

Case
G=(\text{{{let}}}~{x=G^{\prime}}~\text{{{in}}}~g)\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
: Hence
G\langle\iota:z.\ell\rangle=(\text{{{let}}}~{x=G^{\prime}\langle\iota:z.\ell\rangle}~\text{{{in}}}~g)\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta},\ell\mapsto z}
, and

(1)

By Def.
5.6
and typing inversion:

(a)
