{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid y:S^{\,p}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G^{\prime}[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}\,]}:S^{\prime\,p^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}}}
for some
S^{\prime\,p^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}}}
.

(b)

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid y:S^{\,p},x:S^{\prime\,p^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z,{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}}\mathord{*}}\mapsto x)}\,\vdash\,g:T^{\,q^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}
.

(c)

q=q^{\prime}[p^{\prime}/x]
,
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}={\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}[p^{\prime}/x]}
.

(d)

{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}=\operatorname{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime})
for the dependency
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}
at the hole of
G^{\prime}
.

(2)

By IH:
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma,\ell:U^{\,r}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G^{\prime}\langle\iota:z.\ell\rangle[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot}\,]}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\ell}\Rightarrow S^{\prime\,p^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\ell}
.

(3)

By weakening on (1b):
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma,\ell:U^{\,r}\mid y:S^{\,p},x:S^{\prime\,p^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z,({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}}\mathord{*}},{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\ell},{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}r\mathord{*}})\mapsto x)}\,\vdash\,g:T^{\,q^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}
.

(4)

By (
g-let
):
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma,\ell:U^{\,r}\mid y:S^{\,p}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,\text{{{let}}}~{x={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G^{\prime}\langle\iota:z.\ell\rangle[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}\,]}}~\text{{{in}}}~g:T^{\,{q^{\prime}\theta}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\ell}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}\theta

for
\theta=[p^{\prime}/x]
, and the goal follows from that.

∎

Lemma 5.8 (Decomposition).

(1)

If
\ \ {\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}\,]}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot}\,]}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}\Rightarrow T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}

for some
S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
is the dependency at the hole of
G
.

(2)

If
\ \ {\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}\,]}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot}\,]}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}\Rightarrow T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}

for some
S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}
, where

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,b:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\mathord{*}}}}
.

Proof.

Both cases are proved by induction over the respective context.
∎

Lemma 5.9 (Plugging).

(1)

If
\ \ {\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot}\,]}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\Rightarrow T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
is the dependency at the hole of
G
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}\,]}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
.

(2)

If
\ \ {\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot}\,]}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\Rightarrow T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,b:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
, then for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathord{*}}\mapsto z}

it holds that
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}\,]}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
.

Proof.

Both cases are proved by induction over the respective context.
∎

Lemma 5.10 (Qualifier-Growing Replacement).

(1)

If
\ \ {\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}\,]}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\Rightarrow T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,g^{\prime}:S^{\,p,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086},r}
where
\Sigma^{\prime}\supseteq\Sigma
and
r\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime}\setminus\Sigma)
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G^{\prime}[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g^{\prime}}\,]}:T^{\,q,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086},r}
where

G^{\prime}
is the result of contextual effect propagation for the fresh introduction form
r
.

(2)

If
\ \ {\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}\,]}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\Rightarrow T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,b^{\prime}:S^{\,p,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086},r}
where
\Sigma^{\prime}\supseteq\Sigma
and
r\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime}\setminus\Sigma)
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,z.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B^{\prime}[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}\,]}:T^{\,q,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086},r}

where
B^{\prime}
is the result of contextual effect propagation for the fresh introduction form
r
.

Proof.

By decomposition (
Lemma

5.8
), contextual effect propagation (
Lemma

5.7
),
and plugging (
Lemma

5.9
).
∎

5.4.4.
Soundness

Theorem 5.11 (Progress).

If
\ {\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash\,z.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, then either
g
is a location
\ell\in\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)
, or
for any store
\sigma
where
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash\,\sigma
, there exists
a graph term
g^{\prime}
, store
\sigma^{\prime}
, and dependency
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}}
such that
\sigma\mid z.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}}\sigma^{\prime}\mid z.g^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By induction over the typing derivation.
∎

Theorem 5.12 (Preservation).

\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash\,z.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\quad\Sigma\mid\varnothing\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}.z\ \mathsf{ok}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,\sigma\quad\sigma\mid z.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}}\sigma^{\prime}\mid z.g^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}}\end{array}}{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists\Sigma^{\prime}\supseteq\Sigma.\;\exists p\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime}\setminus\Sigma).\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime})}\,\vdash\,z.g^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}}:T^{\,q,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}},p}\\[4.30554pt]
\phantom{\exists\Sigma^{\prime}\supseteq\Sigma.\;\exists p\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime}\setminus\Sigma).\ \ \,\,}\Sigma^{\prime}\mid\varnothing\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}.z\ \mathsf{ok}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime})}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,\sigma^{\prime}\end{array}}

Proof.

By inspecting the rule applied for the step
\sigma\mid z.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}}\sigma^{\prime}\mid z.g^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}}
and
the qualifier-growing replacement
Lemma

5.10
, it is sufficient to prove that
each rule is type/effect/qualifier preserving up to fresh store introductions in a minimal context:

•

Case (
\beta
): In which case we have a well-typed application
\ell_{1}~\ell_{2}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}
in the hole. We proceed by induction
over its typing derivation, which ends either in (
n-app
) or (
b-sub
). No store introduction occurs, hence the context type is preserved.

–

Case (
n-app
):

(1)

We have
\operatorname{cod}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}})\subseteq\{z\}
.

(2)

We have
\ell_{1}:\left(x:T^{\,p\mathord{*}\cap q\mathord{*}}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}U^{\,r}\right)^{\,q}\in{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}
.

(3)

We have
\ell_{2}:T^{\,p}\in{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}
.

(4)

We have
x\notin\operatorname{fv}(U)
,
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\subseteq q,x
,
r\subseteq\varphi,x
, and
\theta=[p/x]
.

(5)

By (1) and environment relation, we have
\sigma(\ell_{1})=\lambda x.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}
.

(6)

By inversion:
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid x:T^{\prime\,p^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,q^{\prime},x}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}x}\,\vdash\,g:U^{\prime\,r^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\mathord{*}}\mapsto x}
,
\Sigma\mid\varnothing\,\vdash\,T^{\,p\mathord{*}\cap q\mathord{*}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}<:T^{\prime\,p^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}
, and
\Sigma\mid x:T^{\,p\mathord{*}\cap q\mathord{*}}\,\vdash\,U^{\prime\,r^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}<:U^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, and
q^{\prime}\subseteq q
.

(7)

By narrowing, weakening, subsumption:
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid x:T^{\,p\mathord{*}\cap q\mathord{*}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma),x}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}x}\,\vdash\,g:U^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.

(8)

By (1) and the substitution and rewiring
Lemma

5.5
:

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,g{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}]}[\ell_{2}/x][\ell_{2}/x]_{\mathsf{t}}:U^{\,r[p/x]}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{[p/x]}.

(9)

By (6) and transitivity, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}\mathord{*}}\mapsto x}
, and hence

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}[x\leadsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}]}[\ell_{2}/x][\ell_{2}/x]_{\mathsf{t}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\,\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}\mathord{*}}{[p/x]}}\mapsto z}

(10)

By (8), (9), and plugging
Lemma

5.9
we can now prove this case.

–

Case (
b-sub
): By IH and subsumption.

•

Case (
let
): Follows from the substitution and rewiring
Lemma

5.5
.

•

Case (
intro
): In which case we have a well-typed let binding
\text{{{let}}}~{x=\iota\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}~\text{{{in}}}~g
in the hole.
By induction over the derivation, only (
b-sub
) or (
g-let
) applies. The first is trivial, and we consider the latter case.
By the contextual effect propagation
Lemma

5.7
, we have increased the qualifiers and effects of the right-hand-side context typing with the fresh new store location,
after which we can proceed as in the (
let
) case with the substitution and rewiring
Lemma

5.5
and conclude.

•

Case (
deref
): In which case we have a well-typed dereference
!~\ell\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}
with
\operatorname{cod}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta})\subseteq\{z\}
in the hole.
By induction over the derivation, only (
b-sub
) or (
n-!
) applies. The first is again trivial, and the latter case is straightforward,
since by the environment predicate we have that thew hole is plugged with a store value of the same type.

•

Case (
assign
): Similar to the previous case.

∎

Corollary 5.13 (Preservation of Separation).

Interleaved executions preserve types and disjointness:

\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\begin{array}[]{l@{\quad}l@{\quad}l@{\quad}l}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\vdash z.g_{1}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}&\sigma\phantom{{}^{\prime}}\mid z.g_{1}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}}\sigma^{\prime}\phantom{{}^{\prime}}\mid z.g_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}^{\prime}}&{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash\,\sigma&\Sigma\mid\varnothing\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}.z\ \mathsf{ok}\\[4.30554pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\vdash z.g_{2}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}&\sigma^{\prime}\mid z.g_{2}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}\mid z.g_{2}^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}^{\prime}}&q_{1}\cap q_{2}\subseteq\varnothing&\end{array}}\end{array}}{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\begin{array}[]{ll@{\quad}l@{\quad}l}\exists p_{1}\;p_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}}}\;\Sigma^{\prime}\;\Sigma^{\prime\prime}.&{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime}\phantom{{}^{\prime}}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime})\phantom{{}^{\prime}}}\,\vdash\,z.g_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}^{\prime}}:T_{1}^{\,p_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}}}&\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\supseteq\Sigma^{\prime}\supseteq\Sigma\\[4.30554pt]
&{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})}\,\vdash\,z.g_{2}^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}^{\prime}}:T_{2}^{\,p_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}}}&p_{1}\cap p_{2}\subseteq\varnothing\end{array}}\end{array}}

Corollary 5.14 (Dependency Safety).

Evaluation respects the order of effect dependencies for well-typed graph IR terms, i.e., an effectful graph node is executed only if all its dependencies
are resolved in the store.

6.
Extension with Soft Dependencies

Graph IR

\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{170654}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{170654}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{119458}{124436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{85327}{88883}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}

-let)

Effect Subtyping

\Sigma\mid\Gamma\,\vdash\,{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}<:{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}

\frac{\Sigma\mid\Gamma\,\vdash\,q_{1}<:q_{2}\hskip 18.49988pt\Sigma\mid\Gamma\,\vdash\,p_{1}<:p_{2}}{\Sigma\mid\Gamma\,\vdash\,{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}(\mathsf{r}:q_{1}\,;\,\mathsf{w}:p_{1})}<:{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}(\mathsf{r}:q_{2};\mathsf{w}:p_{2})}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right=e-sub}

Figure 11.
The syntax and typing rules of the graph IR
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{170654}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{170654}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{119458}{124436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{85327}{88883}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}
with hard and soft dependencies. We only show the changes relative to

Figure

9
. All other rules remain exactly the same using overloaded operations on effects and qualifiers that act component-wise on hard and soft dependencies.

We extend the graph IR
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}
from the previous section with soft dependencies.
During code generation, a node that is only soft-depended by other nodes is
considered dead, and therefore is not scheduled (cf.
Section

9
).
If node
A
hard-depends on node
B
, then
B
must be executed (or scheduled)
before
A
. This is the default notion of dependency for the base
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}
system (
Section

5
), and
entails that no effect operation can be skipped.
This is evidently too rigid, and as motivated in the main paper, soft dependencies gives us more slack
to outright omit effects that are not observable,
e.g.
, write-after-write (WAR) on a mutable reference cell.
If
A

soft-depends
on
B
, then
B
should never be scheduled after

A
, but
B
might not be scheduled even if
A
is scheduled. Being able to tell that some effectful part of a higher-order program can be omitted is immensely
useful.

The entire formal system and reasoning principles of
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}
carry over into a system with hard and
soft dependencies as presented in this section. The difference is the change in the effect and
dependency structure,
i.e.
, effects are split into reads and writes, which induce hard
dependencies (the previous section’s notion) and soft dependencies, respectively. That is to say, we
can regard these new structures as a product composition of the previous with new structures.

In future work, we would like to develop a generic theory of graph IRs that is parametric in such
effect and dependency structures.
Bao et al. 2021
’s direct style system
already proposes one half of the solution by adopting
Gordon 2021
’s effect
quantales. We anticipate that a general graph IR would require a “dependency quantuale” that
mirrors a given effect quantale.

In the following, we focus on the key differences to the previous section.

6.1.
Effects and Dependencies for Reads and Writes

The nature of effects changes from a simple “effectful use of/on a variable” to a more refined
distinction, classifying the effect on the variables as either a read or a write effect. Due to
aliasing/reachability, there is usually more than one variable involved, and compound expressions
accumulate their effects. Thus, we change the effect domain to labelled pairs
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\mathsf{r}:q;\mathsf{w}:p}

of qualifiers, grouping variables/locations by read and write (
Figure

11
). We also
lift the preexisting operations and relations involving effects to such pairs in a straightforward
manner, with the intent that the
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}
typing and synthesis rules can be copied over almost
one-to-one with overloaded notations for the new effect structure. The only tweak needed is that
typing rules introducing effects should classify them as read or write effects, and the
last-use update at let bindings is a bit more involved.

6.2.
Hard-and-Soft Dependency Calculation

We use the hard dependency in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}
to track the last write of any variable/location in context, whereas
its soft dependency tracks all the reads on a variable since it was last written.

Projections need to merge the hard dependencies of a write effect into its soft dependencies, and project the
hard dependencies for reads,
i.e.
,
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{h}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{s}}})|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}(\mathsf{r}:q\,;\,\mathsf{w}:p)}}:=({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{h}}}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}q}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{h}}}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}p}}\sqcup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{s}}}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}p}})
.

Last-use updates at let bindings need to reset the recorded last reads for any written variable, and add the bound variable to the last reads for
any written variable,
i.e.
,

({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{h}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{s}}})\oplus_{x}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}(\mathsf{r}:q\,;\,\mathsf{w}:p)}:=({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{h}},({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}p},x)\mapsto x\ ;\ ({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{s}},({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}p},x)\mapsto\varnothing)\oplus_{x}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}q}}),

where
x
is the let-bound variable and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{s}}\oplus_{x}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}q}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{s}},\{y\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{s}}(y),x\mid y\in{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}q}\}

adds
x
into each set pointed to by
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}q}
.

6.3.
Statics

Figure

11
shows the required changes to the synthesis rules from
Figure

9
(and also indicates
the needed changes for the checking rules from
Figure

8
). With the overloaded operations on effects and dependencies,
the rules for mutable references need to classify their effects on the operands. That is, reference allocations cause a read
on the used allocation capability and its reachable aliases, dereferences a read on the target reference and its aliases, and
assignments a write, accordingly.

6.4.
Metatheory

The theorems and proofs for the graph IR with hard and soft dependency are for the most part identical
to the previous system, and we just repeat the most relevant theorems without proof.

Lemma 6.1 (Synthesis Invariant).

Dependencies are completely determined by the context and effect, as follows:

(1)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\vdash n:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\ \leadsto\bm{n}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}},
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathord{*}}}
.

(2)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\vdash g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\ \leadsto\bm{g}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}},
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}|_{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathord{*}}}
.

Proof.

Analogous to the proof of
Lemma

5.1
.
∎

Lemma 6.2 (Soundness of Synthesis).

Synthesis produces well-typed annotated programs:

(1)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\,\vdash\,n:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\ \leadsto\bm{n}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}},
then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\vdash\bm{n}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.

(2)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\,\vdash\,g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\ \leadsto\bm{g}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}},
then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\vdash\bm{g}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.

Proof.

Analogous to the proof of
Lemma

5.2
.
∎

Lemma 6.3 (Synthesis is Total).

(1)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,n:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
then
\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}.\ \exists{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\bm{n}}.\ \exists{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}.

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\vdash n:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\ \leadsto\bm{n}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\bm{n}}
erases back to
n
.

(2)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
then
\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}.\ \exists{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\bm{g}}.\ \exists{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}.

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}\vdash g:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\ \leadsto\bm{g}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\bm{g}}
erases back to
g
.

Proof.

Analogous to the proof of
Lemma

5.3
.
∎

Corollary 6.4 (End-to-end Type Preservation).

For any well-formed context and compatible
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\Delta}
, there is a type/effect/qualifier-preserving translation from the direct-style
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
-calculus into
the
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}
graph IR with hard and soft dependencies.

Proof.

By lemmas
4.2
,
6.3
, and
6.2
.
∎

Theorem 6.5 (Progress).

If
\ {\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash\,z.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, then either
g
is a location
\ell\in\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)
, or
for any store
\sigma
where
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash\,\sigma
, there exists
a graph term
g^{\prime}
, store
\sigma^{\prime}
, and dependency
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}}
such that
\sigma\mid z.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}}\sigma^{\prime}\mid z.g^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}}
.

Theorem 6.6 (Preservation).

\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash\,z.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\quad\Sigma\mid\varnothing\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}.z\ \mathsf{ok}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,\sigma\quad\sigma\mid z.g\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}}\sigma^{\prime}\mid z.g^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}}\end{array}}{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\exists\Sigma^{\prime}\supseteq\Sigma.\;\exists p\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime}\setminus\Sigma).\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime})}\,\vdash\,z.g^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta}^{\prime}}:T^{\,q,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}},p}\\[4.30554pt]
\phantom{\exists\Sigma^{\prime}\supseteq\Sigma.\;\exists p\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime}\setminus\Sigma).\ \ \,\,}\Sigma^{\prime}\mid\varnothing\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}.z\ \mathsf{ok}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime})}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}\,\vdash\,\sigma^{\prime}\end{array}}

Corollary 6.7 (Preservation of Separation).

Interleaved executions preserve types and disjointness:

\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\begin{array}[]{l@{\quad}l@{\quad}l@{\quad}l}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\vdash g_{1}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}&\sigma\phantom{{}^{\prime}}\mid z.g_{1}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}}\sigma^{\prime}\phantom{{}^{\prime}}\mid z.g_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}^{\prime}}&{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\,\vdash\,\sigma&\Sigma\mid\varnothing\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathchoice{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\displaystyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\textstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptstyle\mapsto$}}{\scalebox{.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mapsto$}}z}.z\ \mathsf{ok}\\[4.30554pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma)}\vdash g_{2}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}&\sigma^{\prime}\mid z.g_{2}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}\mathrel{\longrightarrow_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}\mid z.g_{2}^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}^{\prime}}&q_{1}\cap q_{2}\subseteq\varnothing&\end{array}}\end{array}}{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}{\begin{array}[]{ll@{\quad}l@{\quad}l}\exists p_{1}\;p_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}}}\;\Sigma^{\prime}\;\Sigma^{\prime\prime}.&{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime}\phantom{{}^{\prime}}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime})\phantom{{}^{\prime}}}\,\vdash\,z.g_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{1}}^{\prime}}:T_{1}^{\,p_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}}}&\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\supseteq\Sigma^{\prime}\supseteq\Sigma\\[4.30554pt]
&{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\mid\varnothing{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\operatorname{dom}(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})}\,\vdash\,z.g_{2}^{\prime}\mathbin{\bullet}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\delta_{2}}^{\prime}}:T_{2}^{\,p_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}}}&p_{1}\cap p_{2}\subseteq\varnothing\end{array}}\end{array}}

Corollary 6.8 (Dependency Safety).

Evaluation respects the order of effect dependencies for well-typed graph IR terms, i.e., an effectful graph node is executed only if all its dependencies
are resolved in the store.

7.
Contextual Equivalence - The Direct-Style
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
-Calculus

We apply a logical relations approach following
(
Timany et al. 2022
;
Ahmed et al. 2009
;
Benton et al. 2007
)
to support relational reasoning with respect to the
observational equivalence
of two programs. We define binary logical relations over reachability types (the
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
-calculus in Sec.
2
), and prove the soundness of the equational rules.
Our development is based on a framework for modeling reachability types with logical relations developed in parallel with this work
(
Bao et al. 2023
)
. To make the present report self-contained, pieces of
Bao et al. 2023
are repeated in this section without further reference.
To avoid technical complications, we choose a model that allows mutable references to contain only first-order values, consistent with the previous sections. The definition of the logical relation can be extended to support higher-order references using well-established techniques such as step-indexing
(
Ahmed 2004
;
Ahmed et al. 2009
;
Appel and McAllester 2001
)
, which we leave as future work.

7.1.
High-level Overview of the Proofs

A program
t_{1}
is said to be
contextually equivalent
to another program
t_{2}
, written as
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{ctx}}}t_{2}:T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, if for any program context
C
with a hole of type
T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
,
if
C[t_{1}]
has some (observable) behavior, then so does
C[t_{2}]
.
The definition of context
C
can be found in Sec.
7.2
.

Following the approach of
Timany et al. 2022
and related prior works

(
Ahmed et al. 2009
)
, we define
a judgement for logical equivalence using binary logical relations,
written as
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.

The high-level structure of the proof is the following:

•

Soundness (Theorem
7.43
, Sec.
7.8
). We show that the logical relation is sound with respect to contextual equivalence:

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\text{ implies }\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{ctx}}}t_{2}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}.

•

Compatibility lemmas (Sec.
7.7
). We show that the logical relation is compatible with syntactic typing.

These results can be used to prove the soundness of the equational rules (Sec.
7.9
).

7.2.
Contextual Equivalence

Context for Contextual Equivalence

\begin{array}[]{l@{\ \ }c@{\ \ }l@{\qquad\qquad\ }l@{\ \ }c@{\ \ }l}{C}&::=&\square\mid C\ t\mid t\ C\mid\lambda x.C\mid\text{{{ref}}}_{t}~C\mid\text{{{ref}}}_{C}~t\mid\ !~{C}\mid{C}:={t}\mid{t}:={C}\mid\text{{{let}}}~{x=C}~\text{{{in}}}~t\mid\text{{{let}}}~{x=t}~\text{{{in}}}~C\hfil\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt&&&\\
\end{array}
