(c-let-2)

Figure 12.
Context typing rules for the
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
-Calculus.

Unlike reduction contexts
E
in Fig.
4
, contexts
C
for reasoning about the equivalence allow a “hole” to appear in any place.
We write
C:(\Gamma^{\,\varphi};T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})\Rrightarrow(\Gamma^{\prime\,\varphi^{\prime}};T^{\prime\,q^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}})
to mean that the context
C
is a program of type
T^{\prime\,q^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}
(closed under
\Gamma^{\prime\,\varphi^{\prime}}
) with a hole that can be filled with any program of type
T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
(closed under
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}
).
The typing rules for well-typed contexts imply that if

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
and
C:(\Gamma^{\,\varphi};T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})\Rrightarrow(\Gamma^{\prime\,\varphi^{\prime}};T^{\prime\,q^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}})
hold, then
\Gamma^{\prime\,\varphi^{\prime}}\,\vdash\,C[t]:T^{\prime\,q^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}
.
Fig.
12
shows the typing rules for well-typed contexts.

Two well-typed terms,
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
, under type context
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}
, are
contextually equivalent
if any occurrences of the first term in a closed term can be replaced by the second term without affecting the
observable results
of reducing the program, which is formally defined as follows:

Definition 7.1 (Contextual Equivalence).

We say
t_{1}
is
contextually equivalent
to
t_{2}
, written as
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{ctx}}}t_{2}:T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, if
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{1}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, and
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{2}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, and:

\forall\,C:(\Gamma^{\,\varphi};T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})\Rrightarrow(\varnothing;\mathsf{Unit}^{\,\varnothing}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}).\ C[t_{1}]\downarrow\ \Longleftrightarrow\ C[\,t_{2}\,]\downarrow.

We write
t\downarrow
to mean term
t
terminates, if
\varnothing\mid t\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma\mid v
for some value
v
and final store
\sigma
.

The above definition is standard
(
Ahmed et al. 2009
)
and defines a partial program equivalence.
However, since we focus on a total fragment of the
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
-calculus here, program termination can not be used as an observer for program equivalence.
We will thus rely on the following refined version of contextual equivalence using
Boolean contexts:

\displaystyle\forall\,C:(\Gamma^{\,\varphi};T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})\Rrightarrow(\varnothing;\mathsf{Bool}^{\,\varnothing}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}).\ \exists\ \sigma,\sigma^{\prime},v.

\displaystyle\qquad\varnothing\mid C[t_{1}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma\mid v\;\wedge\;\varnothing\mid C[t_{2}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma^{\prime}\mid v.

That is to say, we consider two terms contextually equivalent if they yield the same answer value in all Boolean contexts.

7.3.
The Model

Following other prior works
(
Thamsborg and Birkedal 2011
;
Benton et al. 2007
;
Ahmed 2004
)
,
we apply Kripke logical relations to the
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
-calculus.
Our logical relations are indexed by types and store layouts via
worlds
.
This allows us to interpret
\mathsf{Ref}\ \mathsf{B}
as an allocated location that holds values of type
\mathsf{B}
.
The invariant that all allocated locations hold well-typed values with respect to the world must hold in the pre-state and be re-established in the post-state of a computation.
The world may grow as more locations may be allocated.
It is important that this invariant must hold in future worlds, which is commonly referred as
monotonicity
.

Considering the restriction to first-order references here, our store layouts are always “flat”,
i.e.
, free of cycles.
The notion of world for the
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
-calculus is defined in the following:

Definition 7.2 (World).

A world
W
is a triple
(L_{1},L_{2},f)
, where

•

L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are finite sets of locations.

•

f\subseteq(L_{1}\times L_{2})
is a partial bijection.

A world is meant to define relational stores.
The partial bijection captures the fact that a relation holds under permutation of locations.

If
\text{W}=(L_{1},L_{2},f)
is a world, we refer to its components as follows:

\begin{array}[]{l l l l}\text{W}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})&=&\begin{cases}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\in f&\text{when defined}\\
\varnothing&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\\
\operatorname{dom}_{1}(\text{W})&=&L_{1}\\
\operatorname{dom}_{2}(\text{W})&=&L_{2}\\
\end{array}

If
W
and
\text{W}^{\prime}
are worlds, such that

\operatorname{dom}_{1}(\text{W})\cap\operatorname{dom}_{1}(\text{W}^{\prime})=\operatorname{dom}_{2}(\text{W})\cap\operatorname{dom}_{2}(\text{W}^{\prime})=\varnothing
,
then
W
and
\text{W}^{\prime}
are called disjoint, and we write
\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}
to mean extending
W
with a disjoint world
\text{W}^{\prime}
.
Let
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
be two stores. We write
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
to mean
\text{W}=(\operatorname{dom}(\sigma_{1}),\operatorname{dom}(\sigma_{2}),f)
.

Our world definition allows us to specify that the domains of two relational stores may grow during a computation, but does not cover store operations, which is important when proving the soundness of equational rules.
Like prior works (
e.g.
,
(
Thamsborg and Birkedal 2011
;
Benton et al. 2007
)
), we use effects as a refinement for the definition of world.
The notation
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
denotes read/write effects, and
\omega
means allocation occurs during a computation.
Local reasoning is enabled by reachability qualifiers and read/write effects,
meaning that what is preserved during an effectful computation are the locations that are
not
mentioned in the read/write effects.
This is a common technique used in reasoning about frames in Hoare-style logics,
e.g.
, separation logic
(
Reynolds 2002
)
.
This treatment is also applicable to our refined effect system (Sec.
6
), where framing is achieved through write effects –
an established technique in Dafny
(
Leino 2010
)
and region logics
(
Banerjee et al. 2013
;
Bao et al. 2015
)
. In this case, a frame indirectly describes the locations that a computation may not change
(
Borgida et al. 1995
)
.
Framing allows the proof to carry properties of effectful terms, such as function applications, since properties that are true for unchanged locations will remain valid
(
Bao et al. 2018
)
.

7.4.
Interpretation of Reachability

Interpretation of Value Reachability

\begin{array}[]{l}\text{locs}({\omega})=\varnothing\hskip 18.49988pt\text{locs}({\mathsf{unit}})=\varnothing\hskip 18.49988pt\text{locs}({c})=\varnothing\hskip 18.49988pt\text{locs}({\ell})=\{\,\ell\,\}\\
\text{locs}({\lambda x.t})=\{\ell\mid\ell\in\operatorname{fv}(t)\land\ell\in\mathsf{Loc}\}\\
\end{array}

Interpretation of Reachability Qualifiers

\begin{array}[]{l@{\ \,}c@{\ \,}l@{\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ }l}\text{locs}({q})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm def}}}{{=}}&\{\,\ell\mid\,\ell\in q\,\land\,\ell\ \in\mathsf{Loc}\}\hfil\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\\
\end{array}

Reachability Predicates

\begin{array}[]{l@{\ \,}c@{\ \,}l@{\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ }l}v\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma}$}}{L}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm def}}}{{=}}&(\operatorname{dom}(\sigma)\,\cap\,\text{locs}({v}))\subseteq L\hfil\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\end{array}

Figure 13.
Interpretation of reachability qualifiers.

In the
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
-calculus, reachability qualifiers are used to specify desired separation or permissible overlapping of reachable locations from a function’s argument and its body.
Fig.
13
shows the interpretation of reachability qualifiers. As in the
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
calculus, values cannot be cyclic, we axiomatize the definition of reachability, without proving termination.
Here, we assume free variables are already substituted with values.

We use
\text{locs}({v})
to define the set of locations that are reachable from a given value
v
.
Base type values,
i.e.
,
\omega
of type
\mathsf{Alloc}
,
\mathsf{unit}
of type
\mathsf{Unit}
, and other constants
c
of other base types
\mathsf{B}
, do not reach any store locations.
Thus, they reach the empty set of locations.
A location
\ell
can only reach itself. Thus, its reachable set is the singleton set
\{\ell\}
.
The set of locations that are reachable from a function value
\lambda x.t
are the set of the locations appearing in the function body.

We overload the function
locs
, and write
\text{locs}({q})
to mean the set of locations reachable from qualifier
q
, which are the set of the locations appeared in
q
.
A bound variable may appear in
q
, and serves as a placeholder to specify the set of locations that a function’s return value may reach.
See Sec.
7.5
for details.
The notation
v\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma}$}}{L}
is a predicate that asserts the set of locations that are reachable from
v
in store
\sigma
is a subset of
L
, where
L
is a set of locations.

7.5.
Binary Logical Relations for
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}

This section presents the definition of binary logical relations for
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
.
Following the approach of
Timany et al. 2022
, we define the binary logical relation for logical equivalence in two steps:

(1)

We define binary interpretations on pairs of closed values, and pairs of closed terms.

(2)

We define the logical equivalence relation on open terms,
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, by lifting the value and term relations to open terms using a closing substitution.

The
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}{}
-calculus has a dependent type system, where types may mention term variables.
We could either define logical relations indexed by two types with variants on qualifiers (
i.e.
, the choice of locations after closing substitution); or indexed by a type where performing closing substitution to qualifiers in the definition.
Here, we choose the latter.
Thus, a relational value substitution is a parameter of the definition of logical relations.

The relational value substitution has to satisfy the context interpretation.
We define the interpretation of typing contexts:

\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{l l l}G[\![{{\varnothing^{\varphi}}}]\!]&=&\varnothing\\
G[\![{{(\Gamma,x:T^{\,q})^{\varphi}}}]\!]&=&\{(\text{W},\gamma;(x\mapsto(v_{1},v_{2})))\mid(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]\,\land\,\varphi\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\Gamma)\,\land\,q\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\Gamma)\,\land\\
&&({\text{W}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{\gamma}\,\land\\
&&(\forall\,q,q^{\prime}.\,q\subseteq\varphi\,\land\,q^{\prime}\subseteq\varphi\,\land\,\Rightarrow\\
&&\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(q\mathord{*})})\,{\cap}\,\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(q^{\prime}\mathord{*})})\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(q\mathord{*}\,{\cap}\,q^{\prime}\mathord{*})})\,\land\\
&&\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(q\mathord{*})})\,{\cap}\,\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(q^{\prime}\mathord{*})})\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(q\mathord{*}\,{\cap}\,q^{\prime}\mathord{*})})))\}\\
\end{array}

In the above definition,
\gamma
ranges over relational value substitutions that are finite maps from variables
x
to pairs of values
(v_{1},v_{2})
.
If
\gamma(x)=(v_{1},v_{2})
, then
\gamma_{1}(x)
denotes
v_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}(x)
denotes
v_{2}
.
We write
\gamma_{1}(q)
and
\gamma_{2}(q)
to mean substituting the free variables in
q
with respect to the relational value substitution
\gamma
.

Value Interpretation of Types and Terms

\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}{}

\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\hspace{1ex}}l@{\hspace{1ex}} l}\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{Alloc}}}]\!]^{\gamma}\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\{({\text{W}},{\omega},{\omega})\}\\
\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{Unit}}}]\!]^{\gamma}\hskip 3.44444pt&=\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\{({\text{W}},{\mathsf{unit}},{\mathsf{unit}})\}\\
\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{Bool}}}]\!]^{\gamma}\hskip 3.44444pt&=\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\{({\text{W}},{v},{v})\mid v=\text{true}\,\lor\,v=\text{false}\}\\
\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{Ref}\ \mathsf{B}}}]\!]^{\gamma}\hskip 3.44444pt&=\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\{({\text{W}},{\ell_{1}},{\ell_{2}})\mid\forall\,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}.\,({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}\,\land\,\ell_{1}\in\operatorname{dom}(\sigma_{1})\,\land\,\ell_{2}\in\operatorname{dom}(\sigma_{2})\,\land\,\text{W}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\,\land\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&({\text{W}},{\sigma_{1}(\ell_{1})},{\sigma_{2}(\ell_{2})})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{B}}}]\!]^{\gamma}\}\\
\hskip 3.44444pt\\
\mathcal{V}[\![{{(x:{T}^{\,p})\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}U^{r}}}]\!]^{\gamma}\hskip 3.44444pt&=\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\{({\text{W}},{\lambda x.t_{1}},{\lambda x.t_{2}})\mid\,\text{locs}({\lambda x.t_{1}})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{1}(\text{W})\,\land\,\text{locs}({\lambda x.t_{2}})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{2}(\text{W})\,\land\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&(\forall v_{1},v_{2},\text{W}^{\prime},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}.({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}}\,\Rightarrow\,({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{\gamma}\,\Rightarrow\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\hskip 17.00024pt\text{locs}({\lambda x.t_{1}})\,{\cap}\,\text{locs}({v_{1}})\,\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(p)})\Rightarrow\text{locs}({\lambda x.t_{2}})\,{\cap}\,\text{locs}({v_{2}})\,\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(p)})\Rightarrow\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\hskip 17.00024pt\exists\,\text{W}^{\prime\prime},\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime}.\,\sigma_{1}\mid t_{1}[x\mapsto v_{1}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\mid v_{1}^{\prime}\,\land\,\sigma_{2}\mid t_{2}[x\mapsto v_{2}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\mid v_{2}^{\prime}\,\land\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt({\sigma_{1}^{\prime}},{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}}\,\land\,({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}},{v_{1}^{\prime}},{v_{2}^{\prime}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{U}}]\!]^{\gamma}\,\land\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt(x\in r\Rightarrow v_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(r)})\cap\text{locs}({\lambda x.t_{1}})\,\cup\,\text{locs}({v_{1}}))}\,\land\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024ptv_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(r)})\cap\text{locs}({\lambda x.t_{2}})\,\cup\,\text{locs}({v_{2}}))})\,\land\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt(x\not\in r\Rightarrow v_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(r)})\cap\text{locs}({\lambda x.t_{1}}))}\,\land\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024ptv_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(r)})\cap\text{locs}({\lambda x.t_{2}}))})\,\land\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt(x\in{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\Rightarrow{\sigma_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}p})})}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\,\land\,{\sigma_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}p})})}{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}})\,\land\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt(x\not\in{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\Rightarrow{\sigma_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})})}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\,\land\,{\sigma_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})})}{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}))\}\\
{\sigma}\hookrightarrow^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip 3.44444pt&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm def}}}{{=}}\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\forall l\in\operatorname{dom}(\sigma).\sigma(l)=\sigma^{\prime}(l)\lor l\in{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\\
\hskip 3.44444pt\\
\mathcal{E}[\![{T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}]\!]^{\gamma}_{{\varphi}}\hskip 3.44444pt&=\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&\{({\text{W}},{t_{1}},{t_{2}})\mid\forall\,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}.({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}\,\land\,\exists\,\text{W}^{\prime},\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime},v_{1},v_{2}.\,t_{1}\mid\sigma_{1}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}v_{1}\mid\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\,\land\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&t_{2}\mid\sigma_{2}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}v_{2}\mid\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\,\land\,({\sigma_{1}^{\prime}},{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}}\,\land\,({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{\gamma}\,\land\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&v_{1}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi\cap q)}))}\,\land\,v_{2}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi\cap q)}))}\,\land\\
\hskip 3.44444pt&\hfil\hskip 3.44444pt&{\sigma_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})})}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\,\land\,{\sigma_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})})}{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\}\\
\end{array}

Figure 14.
Binary value and term interpretation for the
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
-calculus.

The Binary Value Interpretation.

The definition of binary value interpretation of types is shown in Fig.
14
.
The relational interpretation of type
{T}
, written as
\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{\gamma}
, is a set of tuples of form
({\text{W}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})
, where
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
are values, and
W
is a world.
We say
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
are related at type
{T}
with respect to
W
.

Ground Types.

We restrict the base types
B
to
\mathsf{Alloc}
,
\mathsf{Unit}
and
\mathsf{Bool}
to streamline the presentation.
A pair of allocation capabilities
(\omega,\omega)
are related at type
\mathsf{Alloc}
.
A pair of unit values
(\mathsf{unit},\mathsf{unit})
are related at type
\mathsf{Unit}
.
A pair of boolean values are related if they are both
true
or
false
.
A pair of locations
(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})
are related if they are in the domain of the relational store with respect to
W
, (
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
), such that
\text{W}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})
.
It means that a pair of related locations store related values.

Function Types.

Two
\lambda
terms,
\lambda x.t_{1}
and
\lambda x.t_{2}
, are related at type
{T}^{\,p}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}U^{r}
with respect to world
W
, meaning that it satisfies the following conditions:

•

The set of locations reachable from the two
\lambda
terms are well-formed with respect to the world,
i.e.
,
\text{locs}({\lambda x.t_{1}})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{1}(\text{W})
and
\text{locs}({\lambda x.t_{2}})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{2}(\text{W})
.

•

The arguments are allowed if

–

the arguments
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
are related at type
T
with respect
\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}
, for all
\text{W}^{\prime}
; and

–

the overlapping locations reachable from the functions and their arguments are permissible by the argument’s qualifier
p
,
i.e.
,
\text{locs}({\lambda x.t_{1}})\cap\text{locs}({v_{1}})\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(p)})
and
\text{locs}({\lambda x.t_{2}})\cap\text{locs}({v_{2}})\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(p)})
.

•

After substitution, the two terms
t_{1}[x\mapsto v_{1}]
and
t_{2}[x\mapsto v_{2}]
are reduced to some values
v_{1}^{\prime}
and
v_{2}^{\prime}
with some final stores
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
and
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
.

•

\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
and
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
are related with respect to world
\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}
, for some
\text{W}^{\prime\prime}
,
i.e.
,
(\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime}):\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}
.

•

v_{1}^{\prime}
and
v_{2}^{\prime}
are related at type
U
with respect to world
\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}
.

•

If the return value’s qualifier
r
depends on the argument (
i.e.
,
x\in r
), then the locations reachable from
v_{1}^{\prime}
and
v_{2}^{\prime}
are subsets of those reachable both from the function and
r
, plus those reachable from the arguments;
otherwise (
i.e.
,
x\not\in r
), they are just subset of those reachable both from the function and
r
.

•

If a bound variable
x
appears in the effect
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, meaning the function body may modify the argument, then the effect will include the qualifier that may reach the value of function argument
p
; otherwise it is just
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.

The Binary Term Interpretation.

Two related terms,
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
, are defined based on the relation of their computational behaviors,
i.e.
, returned values, reachability qualifiers and effects, which is defined by
\mathcal{E}[\![{{T}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}]\!]^{\gamma}_{{\varphi}}
.
It means for all related stores with respect to world,
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
, if

•

t_{1}
is evaluated to some value
v_{1}
with some final store
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
;

•

t_{2}
is evaluated to some value
v_{2}
with some final store
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
;

•

v_{1}
and
v_{2}
are related at type
T
with respect to world
\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}
for some
\text{W}^{\prime}
;

•

\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
and
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
are related with respect to
\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}
.

•

The locations reachable from the values in the domain of pre-stores are subset of those reachable from
\gamma_{1}(\varphi\cap q)
and
\gamma_{2}(\varphi\cap q)
for each of the term.

•

The effect captures what may be modified in the pre-state store.

Note that we interpret the function body (after substitution) and other terms separately, which allows us to provide more precise reasoning in the logical relations of function types.

Now, we define the binary logical relation for logical equivalence
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
as follows:

Definition 7.3.

\begin{array}[]{l l}\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm def}}}{{=}}\forall(\gamma,\text{W})\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!].({\text{W}},{\gamma_{1}(t_{1})},{\gamma_{2}(t_{2})})\in\mathcal{E}[\![{T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}]\!]^{\gamma}_{{\varphi}}\end{array}

where
\gamma_{1}(t)
and
\gamma_{2}(t)
means substitutions of the free variable in
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
with respect to the relational value substitution
\gamma
.
Closing substitution over qualifiers and effects is performed in the definition of logical relations (Fig.
14
).

7.6.
Metatheory

This section discusses several key lemmas used in the proof of compatibility lemmas (Sec.
7.7
) and soundness of equational rules (Sec.
7.9
).

7.6.1.
World Extension and Relational Stores

Lemma 7.4 (Relational Store Update).

If
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
, and
({\text{W}},{\ell_{1}},{\ell_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{Ref}~\mathsf{B}}}]\!]^{\gamma}
, and

({\text{W}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{B}}}]\!]^{\gamma}
,
then

({\sigma_{1}[\ell_{1}\mapsto v_{1}]},{\sigma_{2}[\ell_{2}\mapsto v_{2}]}):{\text{W}}
.

Proof.

By definition of relational stores.
∎

Lemma 7.5 (Relational Store Extension).

If
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
, and
({\text{W}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{B}}}]\!]^{\gamma}
,
then

({\sigma_{1};(\ell_{1}:v_{1})},{\sigma_{2};{\ell_{2}:v_{2}}}):{\text{W};(\ell_{1},\ell_{2},(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\in f)}
, where
\ell_{1}\not\in\operatorname{dom}(\sigma_{1})
and
\ell_{2}\not\in\operatorname{dom}(\sigma_{2})
.

Proof.

By definition of relational stores.
∎

Lemma 7.6 (Logical Relation Closed Under Relational Value Substitution Extension).

If
T
is closed under
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}
, and
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
, then

({\text{W}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{\gamma}
if and only if
({\text{W}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{{\gamma;\gamma^{\prime}}}
, for all
\gamma^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on type
T
and the constructs of values
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
.
∎

Lemma 7.7 (Logical Relation Closed Under World Extension).

If
({\text{W}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{\gamma}
, then for all
\text{W}^{\prime}
,
({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{\gamma}
.

Proof.

By induction on type
T
and the constructs of values
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
.
∎

7.6.2.
Well-formedness

Lemma 7.8 (Well-formed value interpretation).

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
.
If
({\text{W}},\,{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{\gamma}
, then
\text{locs}({v_{1}})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{1}(\text{W})
and
\text{locs}({v_{2}})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{2}(\text{W})
.

Proof.

By induction on type
T
and the constructs of value
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
.
∎

Lemma 7.9 (Well-formed Typing context interpretation).

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
, then for all
q\subseteq\varphi
,
\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(q)})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{1}(\text{W})
and

\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(q)})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{2}(\text{W})
.

Proof.

By definition of the typing context interpretation and Lemma
7.8
.
∎

Lemma 7.10.

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
, then
\operatorname{dom}(\gamma_{1})=\operatorname{dom}(\gamma_{2})=\operatorname{dom}(\Gamma)
, and
\operatorname{dom}(\Gamma)\mathord{*}
.

Proof.

Immediately by the definition of typing context interpretation and the definition of saturation in Fig.
3
.
∎

7.6.3.
Semantic Typing Context

Lemma 7.11 (Semantic Typing Context Tighten).

If
(\text{W},\gamma,)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
, then for all
p\subseteq\varphi
,
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,p}}}]\!]
.

Proof.

By the definition of typing context interpretation.
∎

Lemma 7.12 (Semantic Typing Context Extension 1).

If
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
,
and
q\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\Gamma)
,
and
({\text{W}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{\gamma}
,
and
\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi)})\cap\text{locs}({v_{1}})\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(q)})
,
and
\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi)})\cap\text{locs}({v_{2}})\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(q)})
,
then
(\text{W},\gamma;(x\mapsto(v_{1},v_{2})))\in G[\![{{(\Gamma,x:T^{\,q})^{\varphi,x}}}]\!]

Proof.

By typing context interpretation and Lemma
7.6
.
∎

Lemma 7.13 (Semantic Typing Context Extension 2).

If
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
,
and
({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{\gamma}
,
and
\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(q)})\cap\text{locs}({v_{1}})\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(p)})
,
and
\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(q)})\cap\text{locs}({v_{2}})\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(p)})
,
and
q\subseteq\varphi
,
then
(\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime},\gamma;(x\mapsto(v_{1},v_{2})))\in G[\![{{(\Gamma,x:T^{\,p})^{q,x}}}]\!]
.

Proof.

By typing context interpretation, Lemma
7.6
, Lemma
7.7
and Lemma
7.11
.
∎

7.6.4.
Reachability Qualifiers

Lemma 7.14.

For all
\sigma
,
p
and
q
,
\omega\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma}$}}{\text{locs}({p\cap q})}
.

Proof.

Immediate by the definition in Fig.
13
.
∎

Lemma 7.15.

For all
\sigma
,
p
and
q
,
\mathsf{unit}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma}$}}{\text{locs}({p\cap q})}
.

Proof.

Immediate by the definition in Fig.
13
.
∎

Lemma 7.16.

For all
\sigma
,
b
,
p
and
q
,
b\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma}$}}{\text{locs}({p\cap q})}
, where
b
is
true
or
false
.

Proof.

Immediate by the definition in Fig.
13
.
∎

Lemma 7.17.

For all
\sigma
,
\ell
,
p
and
q
,
\ell\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma}$}}{\text{locs}({p\cap q})}
, where
\ell\not\in\operatorname{dom}(\sigma)
.

Proof.

Immediate by the definition in Fig.
13
.
∎

Lemma 7.18.

If
(\Gamma,x:T^{\,p})^{q,x}\,\vdash\,t:U^{\,r}
, then
for all
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{(\Gamma,x:T^{\,p})^{q,x}}}]\!]
,

\gamma_{1}(\lambda x.t)\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\operatorname{dom}_{1}(\text{W})}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(q)})}
and

\gamma_{2}(\lambda x.t)\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\operatorname{dom}_{2}(\text{W})}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(q)})}
.

Proof.

By the syntactic structure that ensures
q
contains all the free variables in
t
,
it is obvious that after substituting free variables with values, the conclusions hold.
∎

7.6.5.
Effects

Here we introduce several notations to streamline the presentation.

Let
\sigma
be a store. We write
\sigma=\sigma_{1}\ast\sigma_{2}
(for some
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
) to denote that store
\sigma
can be split into two disjoint parts
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
.

Let
\sigma
be a store and
L
be a set of locations.
We write
({\sigma}\!\!\downarrow\!\!{L})
to mean localizing a partial store with respect to
L
,
meaning

\operatorname{dom}(({\sigma}\!\!\downarrow\!\!{L}))=\operatorname{dom}(\sigma)\cap L\>\land\>\forall\>\ell\in\operatorname{dom}(({\sigma}\!\!\downarrow\!\!{L})).({\sigma}\!\!\downarrow\!\!{L})(\ell)=\sigma(\ell)
.

Lemma 7.19 (Read/Write Effects).

If

\ell\in\operatorname{dom}(\sigma)
,
and

\ell\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma}$}}{\text{locs}({p\cap q})}
,
then

{\sigma}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({q})}{\sigma[\ell\mapsto v]}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.17
and interpretation of effects.
∎

Lemma 7.20 (No Effects).

{\sigma}\hookrightarrow^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}}{\sigma}
.

Proof.

Immediate by the definition of effects.
∎

Lemma 7.21 (SubEffects).

If
\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}})\subseteq\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}})
,
and
{\sigma}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}})}{\sigma^{\prime}}
,
then
{\sigma}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}})}{\sigma^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By the interpretation of effects.
∎

Lemma 7.22 (Effects Composition).

If
{\sigma}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}})}{\sigma^{\prime}}
,
and
{\sigma^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}})\mathord{*}})}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}\mathord{*}=\varnothing
,
and
\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\sigma)
,
and
\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}\mathord{*}})\cap\operatorname{dom}(\sigma)=\varnothing
.
then
{\sigma}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})\mathord{*}})}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}

Proof.

By the interpretation of effects.
∎

Lemma 7.23 (Framing).

If
{\sigma}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}})}{\sigma^{\prime}}
,
then
{\sigma}\!\downarrow\!{(\operatorname{dom}(\sigma)-\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathord{*}}))}={\sigma^{\prime}}\!\downarrow\!{(\operatorname{dom}(\sigma)-\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathord{*}}))}

Proof.

By the interpretation of observable effects: the set of locations that may be written in the reduction of
t
must be in
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.
Thus, the values stored in the locations
\sigma
, but are separate from
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathord{*}
must be preserved.
∎

Lemma 7.24 (Effect Separation).

If
\sigma\mid t_{1}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma^{\prime}\mid v_{1}
,
and
\sigma\mid t_{2}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}\mid v_{2}
,
and
{\sigma}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}})}{\sigma^{\prime}}
,
and
{\sigma}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}})}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}=\varnothing
,
then

{\sigma^{\prime}}\!\downarrow\!{(\operatorname{dom}(\sigma)-\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}})-\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}))}={\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\!\downarrow\!{(\operatorname{dom}(\sigma)-\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}})-\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}))}
.

Proof.

Let

\sigma_{1}={\sigma}\!\downarrow\!{\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}})}
,
and
\sigma_{2}={\sigma}\!\downarrow\!{\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}})}
,
and
\sigma_{3}={\sigma}\!\downarrow\!{(\operatorname{dom}(\sigma)-\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}})-\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}))}
,
We know that
\sigma=\sigma_{1}\ast\sigma_{2}\ast\sigma_{3}
, as
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}=\varnothing
.

By
{\sigma}\hookrightarrow^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}}{\sigma^{\prime}}

and Lemma
7.23
, we know
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{2}\ast\sigma_{3}\ast\sigma_{fr1}
, for some
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
, where
\sigma_{fr1}\ast\sigma
.

By
{\sigma}\hookrightarrow^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}

and Lemma
7.23
, we know
\sigma^{\prime\prime}=\sigma_{1}\ast\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{3}\ast\sigma_{fr2}
, for some
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
, where
\sigma_{fr2}\ast\sigma
.

Then
{\sigma^{\prime}}\!\downarrow\!{(\operatorname{dom}(\sigma)-\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}})-\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}))}=\sigma_{3}
,
and
{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\!\downarrow\!{(\operatorname{dom}(\sigma)-\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}})-\text{locs}({{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}}))}=\sigma_{3}
.
∎

7.6.6.
Other auxiliary lemmas

Lemma 7.25 (Qualifier intersection distributes over locations).

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
, and
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
.
For all
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
,
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
and
\text{W}^{\prime}
, such that
({\sigma_{1}^{\prime}},{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}}
if

v_{f1}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(q_{f})})}
,
and
v_{f2}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(q_{f})})}
,
and
v_{1}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(p)})}
,
and
v_{2}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(p)})}
,
and
\text{locs}({v_{f1}})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime})
,
and
\text{locs}({v_{f2}})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\sigma_{2}^{\prime})
,
then

(\text{locs}({v_{f1}})\,{\cap}\,\text{locs}({v_{1}}))\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(p\mathord{*}\cap q_{f}\mathord{*})})

and
(\text{locs}({v_{f2}})\,{\cap}\,\text{locs}({v_{2}}))\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(p\mathord{*}\cap q_{f}\mathord{*})})
.

Proof.

By typing context interpretation, Lemma
7.9
and set theory.
∎

Lemma 7.26 (Semantic Function Abstraction).

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
,
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
, and
\operatorname{dom}(\Gamma)\mathord{*}
.
For all
\text{W}^{\prime}
, if

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},\,{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{\gamma}
,
and
\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\lambda x.t_{1})})\cap\text{locs}({v_{1}})\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(p)})
,
and
\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\lambda x.t_{2})})\cap\text{locs}({v_{2}})\subseteq\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(p)})
,
and
(\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime},\gamma;x\mapsto(v_{1},v_{2}))\in G[\![{{(\Gamma,x:T^{\,p})^{q,x}}}]\!]
implies
that there exists
\text{W}^{\prime\prime}
, such that

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}},\,{\gamma_{1}(t_{1})[x\mapsto v_{1}]},{\gamma_{2}(t_{2})[x\mapsto v_{2}]})\in\mathcal{E}[\![{U^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}]\!]^{\gamma}_{{q,x}}
.
and
p\subseteq q
,
then exists
\text{W}^{\prime\prime}
,
v_{1}^{\prime}
,
v_{2}^{\prime}
, such that

(1)

\sigma_{1}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{1})[x\mapsto v_{1}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\mid v_{2}

(2)

\sigma_{2}\mid\gamma_{2}(t_{2})[x\mapsto v_{2}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\mid v_{2}^{\prime}

(3)

({\sigma_{1}^{\prime}},{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}}

(4)

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}},\,{v_{3}},{v_{4}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{U}}}]\!]^{\gamma}

(5)

(x\in r\Rightarrow v_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(r)})\cap\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\lambda x.t_{1})})\,\cup\,\text{locs}({v_{1}}))}\,\land

\qquad\qquad v_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(r)})\cap\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\lambda x.t_{2})})\,\cup\,\text{locs}({v_{2}}))})

(6)

(x\not\in r\Rightarrow v_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(r)})\cap\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\lambda x.t_{1})}))}\,\land\,v_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(r)})\cap\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\lambda x.t_{2})}))})

Proof.

By Lemma
7.13
,
(\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime},\gamma;(x\mapsto(v_{1},v_{2})))\in G[\![{{(\Gamma,x:T^{\,p})^{q,x}}}]\!]
. Thus, there exists
\text{W}^{\prime\prime}
, such that
({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}},\,{\gamma_{1}(t_{1})[x\mapsto v_{1}]},{\gamma_{2}(t_{2})[x\mapsto v_{2}]})\in\mathcal{E}[\![{U^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}]\!]^{\gamma}_{{q,x}}
, which can be used to prove (1) - (4).
(5) and (6) can be proved by inspecting
x\in r
, Lemma
7.8
and Lemma
7.18
.
∎

Lemma 7.27 (Semantic Application).

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
.
If

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},\,{\gamma_{1}(\lambda x.t_{1})},{\gamma_{2}(\lambda x.t_{2})})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{T}^{\,p\mathord{*}\cap q\mathord{*}}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}U^{r}}}]\!]^{\gamma}
,
and
\gamma_{1}(\lambda x.t_{1})\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(q)})}
,
and
\gamma_{2}(\lambda x.t_{2})\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(q)})}
,
and
({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}},\,{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{\gamma}
,
and
v_{1}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\operatorname{dom}_{1}(\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime})}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(p)})}
,
and
v_{2}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\operatorname{dom}_{2}(\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime})}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(p)})}
,
and
r\subseteq\varphi,x
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\subseteq q,x
,
and
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}}

then there exists
v_{2}
,
v_{2}^{\prime}
,
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
,
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
,
\text{W}^{\prime\prime\prime}
, such that

(1)

\sigma_{1}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{1})[x\mapsto v_{1}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\mid v_{2}
;

(2)

\sigma_{2}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{2})[x\mapsto v_{2}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\mid v_{2}^{\prime}
;

(3)

({\sigma_{1}^{\prime}},{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime\prime}}
;

(4)

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime\prime}},\,{v_{2}},{v_{2}^{\prime}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{U}}]\!]^{\gamma}
;

(5)

x\in r\Rightarrow v_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(r)})\cap\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\lambda x.t_{1})})\,\cup\,\text{locs}({v_{1}}))}\,\land

\;\;\;v_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(r)})\cap\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\lambda x.t_{2})})\,\cup\,\text{locs}({v_{2}}))}

(6)

x\not\in r\Rightarrow v_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(r)})\cap\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\lambda x.t_{1})}))}\,\land\,v_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(r)})\cap\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\lambda x.t_{2})}))}

Proof.

By Lemma
7.8
, we know the following:

•

\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\lambda x.t_{1})})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{1}(\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime})
;

•

\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\lambda x.t_{2})})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{2}(\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime})
;

•

\text{locs}({v_{1}})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{1}(\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime})
;

•

\text{locs}({v_{2}})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{2}(\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime})
;

Then (1) - (4) can be proved by the assumption
({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},\,{\gamma_{1}(\lambda x.t_{1})},{\gamma_{2}(\lambda x.t_{2})})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{T}^{\,p}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}U^{r}}}]\!]^{\gamma}
, and
({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}},\,{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T}}]\!]^{\gamma}
, and Lemma
7.25
.
(5) - (6) can be proved by inspecting
x\in r
.
∎

7.7.
Compatibility Lemmas

The following compatibility lemmas show that the logical relations is
compatible
with all the constructs of the language
(
Pierce 2004
)
.

Lemma 7.28 (Compatibility:
\mathsf{Alloc}
).

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models\omega\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}\omega:Alloc^{\,\varnothing}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}

Proof.

By the typing context interpretation, value interpretation in Fig.
14
and Lemma
7.14
.
∎

\mathsf{Unit}
).

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models\mathsf{unit}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}\mathsf{unit}:\mathsf{Unit}^{\,\varnothing}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}

By the typing context interpretation, value interpretation in Fig.
14
and Lemma
7.15
.
∎

\mathsf{Bool}
