Lemma 7.32 (Compatibility: Variables).

If
x:T^{\,q}\in\Gamma
and
x\subseteq\varphi
, then

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models x\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}x:T^{\,x}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}

Proof.

Immediate by the typing context interpretation in Fig.
14
.
∎

Lemma 7.33 (Compatibility:
\lambda
).

If
(\Gamma\ ,\ x:T^{\,p})^{q,x}\ \models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:U^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
,
q\subseteq\varphi
,
then

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models\lambda x.t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}\lambda x.t_{2}:\left(x:T^{\,p}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}U^{\,r}\right)^{\,q}\;{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}
.

Proof.

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma}}]\!]
and
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
.

By definition of term interpretation, we need to show there exists
\text{W}^{\prime}
,
\sigma^{\prime}
,
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
such that:

(1)

\sigma_{1}\mid\gamma_{1}(\lambda x.t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\mid v_{1}

(2)

\sigma_{2}\mid\gamma_{2}(\lambda x.t_{2})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\mid v_{2}

(3)

({\sigma^{\prime}_{1}},{\sigma^{\prime}_{2}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}}

(4)

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},\,{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{{\left(x:T^{\,p}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}U^{\,r}\right)}}}]\!]^{\gamma}

(5)

v_{1}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi\cap q)})}

(6)

v_{2}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi\cap q)})}

(7)

{\sigma_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}

(8)

{\sigma_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}}{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}

By reduction semantics, we pick
\text{W}=\varnothing
,
v_{1}=\lambda x.t_{1}
,
v_{2}=\lambda x.t_{2}
,
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}=\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}=\sigma_{2}
.
Thus, (1)- (3) are discharged.
(4) can be proved by Lemma
7.10
and Lemma
7.26
.
(5) and (6) can be proved by Lemma
7.18
.
(7) and (8) can be proved by Lemma
7.20
.
∎

Lemma 7.34 (Compatibility : Allocation).

If
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:\mathsf{Alloc}^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
, and
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{3}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{4}:B^{\,\varnothing}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
, then

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models\text{{{ref}}}_{t_{1}}~t_{3}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}\text{{{ref}}}_{t_{2}}~t_{4}\ :(\mathsf{Ref}\ B)^{\,\varnothing}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}q}
.

Proof.

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma}}]\!]
and
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
.
By the first assumption, we know that there exists
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
,
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
,
\text{W}^{\prime}
,
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
, such that

•

\gamma_{1}(t_{1})\mid\sigma_{1}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}v_{1}\mid\sigma_{1}^{\prime}

•

\gamma_{2}(t_{2})\mid\sigma_{2}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}v_{2}\mid\sigma_{2}^{\prime}

•

({\sigma_{1}^{\prime}},{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}}

•

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{Alloc}}}]\!]^{\gamma}

•

v_{1}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi\cap q)})}

•

v_{2}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi\cap q)})}

•

{\sigma_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})})}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}

•

{\sigma_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})})}{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}

By reduction semantics, we know
v_{1}=v_{2}=\omega
.

By the second assumption, we know that there exists
\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}
,
\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}
,
\text{W}^{\prime\prime}
,
v_{3}
and
v_{4}
, such that

•

\gamma_{1}(t_{3})\mid\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}v_{3}\mid\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}

•

\gamma_{2}(t_{4})\mid\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}v_{4}\mid\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}

•

({\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}},{\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}}

•

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}},{v_{3}},{v_{4}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{B}}}]\!]^{\gamma}

•

v_{3}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi\cap\varnothing)})}

•

v_{4}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi\cap\varnothing)})}

•

{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})})}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}}

•

{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})})}{\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}}

By reduction semantics, we know

•

\text{{{ref}}}_{\omega}~v_{3}\mid\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{1}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\ell_{1}\mid\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime};(\ell_{1}\mapsto v_{3})
, where
\ell_{1}\not\in\operatorname{dom}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime})

•

\text{{{ref}}}_{\omega}~v_{4}\mid\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{1}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\ell_{2}\mid\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime};(\ell_{2}\mapsto v_{4})
, where
\ell_{2}\not\in\operatorname{dom}(\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime})

By Lemma
7.5
, we know
({\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime};(\ell_{1}\mapsto v_{3})},{\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime};(\ell_{2}\mapsto v_{4})}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime};((\ell_{1}\mapsto v_{3}),(\ell_{2}\mapsto v_{4}),\{(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\})}
.
The rest of the proof can be done by the definition of value interpretation, Lemma
7.22
and Lemma
7.17
.
∎

Lemma 7.35 (Compatibility: Dereference (
!
)).

If
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:(\mathsf{Ref}~B)^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, then
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models!t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}!t_{2}:B^{\,\varnothing}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{q}}
.

Proof.

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
and
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
.
By the assumption,
({\text{W}},{\gamma_{1}(t_{1})},{\gamma_{2}(t_{2})})\in\mathcal{E}[\![{\mathsf{Ref}~B^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}]\!]^{\gamma}_{{\varphi}}
,
and reduction semantics, we know there exists
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
,
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
,
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
such that

•

\sigma_{1}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\mid\ell_{1}

•

\sigma_{2}\mid\gamma_{2}(t_{2})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\mid\ell_{2}

•

({\sigma_{1}^{\prime}},{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}}

•

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},{\ell_{1}},{\ell_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{Ref}\ B}}]\!]^{\gamma}

•

\ell_{1}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi\cap q)})}

•

\ell_{2}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi\cap q)})}

•

{\sigma_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})})}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}

•

{\sigma_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})})}{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}

We can finish the proof by reduction semantics, value interpretation, Lemma
7.16
, Lemma
7.21
,
where we pick
\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}
to be
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
,
\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}
to be
\sigma^{\prime}
, and
\text{W}^{\prime\prime}
to be
\varnothing
.

∎

Lemma 7.36 (Compatibility: Assignments (
:=
)).

If
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:(\mathsf{Ref}~\mathsf{B})^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
,

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{3}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{4}:\mathsf{B}^{\,\varnothing}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
,
then
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}:=t_{3}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:=t_{4}:\mathsf{Unit}^{\,\varnothing}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{q}}
.

Proof.

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
and
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
.
By the first assumption, we know that there exists
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
,
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
,
\text{W}^{\prime}
,
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
such that

•

\gamma_{1}(t_{1})\mid\sigma_{1}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}v_{1}\mid\sigma_{1}^{\prime}

•

\gamma_{2}(t_{2})\mid\sigma_{2}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}v_{2}\mid\sigma_{2}^{\prime}

•

({\sigma_{1}^{\prime}},{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}}

•

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},{v_{1}},{v_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{Ref}~\mathsf{B}}}]\!]^{\gamma}

•

v_{1}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi\cap q)})}

•

v_{2}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi\cap q)})}

•

{\sigma_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})})}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}

•

{\sigma_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})})}{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}

By the second assumption, we know that there exists
\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}
,
\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}
,
\text{W}^{\prime\prime}
,
v_{3}
and
v_{4}
, such that

•

\gamma_{1}(t_{3})\mid\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}v_{3}\mid\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}

•

\gamma_{2}(t_{4})\mid\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}v_{4}\mid\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}

•

({\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}},{\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}}

•

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}},{v_{3}},{v_{4}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{B}}}]\!]^{\gamma}

•

v_{3}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi\cap\varnothing)})}

•

v_{4}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}$}}{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi\cap\varnothing)})}

•

{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})})}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}}

•

{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})})}{\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}}

Then the proof can be done by the reduction semantics, Lemma
7.4
, value interpretation, Lemma
7.15
, Lemma
7.19
and Lemma
7.22
.

∎

Lemma 7.37 (Compatibility: Applications (
\beta
)).

. If
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:\left(x{\,:\,}T^{\,p\mathord{*}\cap q\mathord{*}}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}}U^{\,r}\right)^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
, and

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{3}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{4}:T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
, and
x\notin\operatorname{fv}(U)
,
and
r\subseteq\varphi,x
, and
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}\subseteq\varphi,x
, and
\theta=[p/x]
,
then

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}~t_{3}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}~t_{4}:(U^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}})\theta
.

Proof.

The proof is done by the definition of term interpretation, Lemma
7.27
and Lemma
7.22
.
∎

Lemma 7.38 (Compatibility: Let).

If
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
,
and
(\Gamma\,,\,x:S^{\,p\mathord{*}\cap\varphi\mathord{*}})^{\varphi,x}\models t_{3}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{4}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
,
and
\theta=[p/x]

and
\quad x\notin\operatorname{fv}{(T)}
,
then
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models\text{{{let}}}~{x=t_{1}}~\text{{{in}}}~t_{3}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}\text{{{let}}}~{x=t_{2}}~\text{{{in}}}~t_{4}:(T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}})\theta

Proof.

Since the
t-let
is a combination of rules
t-abs
,
t-app
and weakening, the proof is analogous.
∎

Lemma 7.39 (Compatibility: Subtyping).

If
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}

and
\Gamma\,\vdash\,S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}<:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}

and
q,{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\subseteq\varphi
,
then
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the subtyping derivation.
∎

7.8.
The Fundamental Theorem and Soundness

Theorem 7.40 (Fundamental Property).

If
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, then
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
. Each case follows from the corresponding compatibility lemma.
∎

Lemma 7.41 (Congruency of Binary Logical Relations).

The binary logical relation is closed under well-typed program contexts,
i.e., if
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
,
and
C:(\Gamma^{\,\varphi};T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})\Rrightarrow(\Gamma^{\prime\,\varphi^{\prime}};T^{\prime\,p^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\prime})
, then
\Gamma^{\prime\,\varphi^{\prime}}\models C[t_{1}]\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}C[t_{2}]:T^{\prime\,p^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of context
C
. Each case follows from the corresponding compatibility lemma and may use the fundamental theorem (Theorem
7.40
) if necessary.
∎

Lemma 7.42 (Adequacy of the binary logical relations).

The binary logical relation preserves termination, i.e., if
\varnothing\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:T^{\,\varnothing}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}
,
then
\exists\ \sigma,\sigma^{\prime},v.\ \varnothing\mid t_{1}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma\mid v\wedge\varnothing\mid t_{2}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma^{\prime}\mid v
.

Proof.

We know
(\varnothing,\varnothing)\in G[\![{{\varnothing}}]\!]
by the interpretation of typing context.
Then we can prove the result by the binary term interpretation (Fig.
14
).
∎

Theorem 7.43 (Soundness of Binary Logical Relations).

The binary logical relation is sound w.r.t. contextually equivalence, i.e.,
if
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{1}:T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
and
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{2}:T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
, then

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
implies
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{ctx}}}t_{2}:T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
.

Proof.

By the refined definition of contextual equivalence, to prove the result, we are given a well-typed context
C:(\Gamma^{\,\varphi};T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})\Rrightarrow(\varnothing;B^{\,\varnothing}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}})
,
and we need to show
\exists\ \sigma,\sigma^{\prime},v.\ \varnothing\mid C[t_{1}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma\mid v\wedge\varnothing\mid C[t_{2}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma^{\prime}\mid v
. By the assumption, and the congruency lemma (Lemma
7.41
), we have
\varnothing\models C[t_{1}]\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}C[t_{2}]:B^{\,\varnothing}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}
, which
leads to
\exists\ \sigma,\sigma^{\prime},v.\ \varnothing\mid C[t_{1}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma\mid v\wedge\varnothing\mid C[t_{2}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma^{\prime}\mid v
by the adequacy lemma (Lemma
7.42
).
∎

7.9.
Equational Rules

\frac{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\quad\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{2}:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\quad t_{1}\text{ terminates}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}\ \text{or}\ \omega}{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models\text{{{let}}}~{x=t_{1}}~\text{{{in}}}~t_{2}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}}\quad\textsc{left=dce}\par\frac{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\quad\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{2}:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Gamma\ ,\ x:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}\mathord{*}\cap\varphi\mathord{*}},\ y:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}\mathord{*}\cap(\varphi,x)\mathord{*}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi,x,y}\,\vdash\,t:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}=\varnothing\quad x\not\in\operatorname{fv}(T)\quad y\not\in\operatorname{fv}(T)\quad\theta=[q_{2}/y][q_{1}/x]}{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models\text{{{let}}}~x=t_{1}~\text{{{in}}}~\text{{{let}}}~y=t_{2}~\text{{{in}}}~t\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}\text{{{let}}}~y=t_{2}\ \text{{{in}}}\ \text{{{let}}}\ x=t_{1}~\text{{{in}}}~t:(T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})\theta}\quad\textsc{left=comm}\par\frac{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Gamma\ ,\ x:T^{\,p},y:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}\mathord{*}\cap(\varphi,x)\mathord{*}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,q,x,y}\,\vdash\,\ t:U^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\quad\theta=[q_{1}/y]\quad x\not\in\operatorname{fv}(U)\quad y\not\in\operatorname{fv}(U)}{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models(\lambda x:T^{\,p}.\ \text{{{let}}}\ y=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ t)\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}(\text{{{let}}}\ y=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ \lambda x:T^{\,p}.t):(x:T^{\,p}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}U^{\,r}\theta)^{q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}}\quad\textsc{left=$\lambda$-hoist}\par\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Gamma,x:T^{\,p}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,q,x}\,\vdash\,t_{1}:U^{\,r}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\quad\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{2}:T^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}\quad x\notin\operatorname{fv}(U)\quad\theta=[p/x]}{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models(\lambda x:T^{\,p}.t_{1})(t_{2})\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{1}[t_{2}/x]:(U^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})\theta}\quad\textsc{left=$\beta$-inlining}\par\frac{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Gamma\ ,\ x:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}\mathord{*}\cap\varphi\mathord{*}},y:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}\mathord{*}\cap(\varphi,x)\mathord{*}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi,x,y}\,\vdash\,\ t:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}\quad\omega\notin{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\quad\theta=[x/y]}{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models(\text{{{let}}}\ x=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ \text{{{let}}}\ y=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ t)\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}(\text{{{let}}}\ x=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ t\ \theta):(T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})\theta\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}}\\
}\quad\textsc{left = e-cse}\par

Figure 15.
Equational rules for the
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
-calculus.

Fig.
15
shows equational rules for
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
with effects specifying logically equivalent terms.
Rule (
dce
) permits the removal of a terminating term,
t_{1}
, whose computation result is an unused value, provided that the effect of that computation only allows allocation.
Removing a term with effects may not be sound, as the effects could be observed by the following computations.
Rule (
comm
) permits re-ordering of two terms if their effects are separate, which entails disjoint sets of store locations (Corollary
2.13
).
Rule (
\lambda
-hoist
) permits a pure computation to be moved out of the abstraction boundary.
Rule (
\beta
-inlining
) permits replacing a function call site
t_{2}
with the body of the called function, provided that
t_{2}
is pure.
Rule (
e-cse
) permits removing a duplicated computation, provided that no fresh allocations occur during the reduction of the term.
The rest of this section shows the proofs of those equational rules by using our logical relations.

Lemma 7.44 (Dead Code Elimination).

If

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
,
and
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{2}:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}
or
\omega
,
and
t_{1}
terminates,
then

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,\text{{{let}}}~{x=t_{1}}~\text{{{in}}}~t_{2}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
.

Proof.

By the fundamental property (Theorem
7.40
), we know on the first assumption, we know

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
.

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
and
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
.
By the definition of binary logical relations and binary term interpretation, we know there exists
\sigma_{11}
,
\sigma_{12}
,
\text{W}_{1}
,
v_{11}
and
v_{12}
, such that

•

\sigma_{1}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{11}\mid v_{11}

•

\sigma_{2}\mid\gamma_{2}(t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{12}\mid v_{12}

•

({\text{W};\text{W}_{1}},{v_{11}},{v_{12}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{T_{1}}}}]\!]^{\gamma}

•

({\sigma_{11}},{\sigma_{12}}):{\text{W};\text{W}_{1}}

•

v_{11}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi\cap q_{1})}))}

•

v_{12}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi\cap q_{1})}))}

•

{\sigma_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})})}{\sigma_{11}}

•

{\sigma_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})})}{\sigma_{12}}

By
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}
or
\omega
, we know

\sigma_{11}=\sigma_{1}\ast\sigma_{fr1}
, and
\sigma_{12}=\sigma_{2}\ast\sigma_{fr2}
.

By the fundamental property (Theorem
7.40
) again, we know

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{2}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
.

By the binary term interpretation, we know
there exists
\sigma_{21}
,
\sigma_{22}
,
\text{W}_{2}
,
v_{21}
and
v_{22}
, such that

•

\sigma_{1}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{2})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{21}\mid v_{21}

•

\sigma_{2}\mid\gamma_{2}(t_{2})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{22}\mid v_{22}

•

({\text{W};\text{W}_{2}},{v_{21}},{v_{22}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{T_{2}}}}]\!]^{\gamma}

•

({\sigma_{21}},{\sigma_{22}}):{\text{W};\text{W}_{2}}

•

v_{21}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi\cap q_{2})}))}

•

v_{22}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi\cap q_{2})}))}

•

{\sigma_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})})}{\sigma_{11}}

•

{\sigma_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})})}{\sigma_{12}}

From the left, by the reduction semantics, we have:

•

\sigma_{1}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{1}\ast\sigma_{fr1}\mid v_{11}

•

\sigma_{1}\ast\sigma_{fr1}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{2})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{21}\mid v_{21}^{\prime}

By the deterministic of reduction semantics, we know
v_{21}=v_{21}^{\prime}
.

Form the right, by the reduction semantics, we know
\sigma_{2}\mid\gamma_{2}(t_{2})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{22}\mid v_{22}
.

By the fact we have above, the proof is done.

∎

Lemma 7.45 (comm).

If

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
,
and
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{2}:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Gamma\ ,\ x:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}\mathord{*}\cap\varphi\mathord{*}},\ y:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}\mathord{*}\cap(\varphi,x)\mathord{*}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi,x,y}\,\vdash\,t:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*}=\varnothing
,
and
x\not\in\operatorname{fv}(T)
,
and
y\not\in\operatorname{fv}(T)
,
and
\theta=[q_{2}/y][q_{1}/x]
,
then

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models\text{{{let}}}~x=t_{1}~\text{{{in}}}~\text{{{let}}}~y=t_{2}~\text{{{in}}}~t\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}\text{{{let}}}~y=t_{2}\ \text{{{in}}}\ \text{{{let}}}\ x=t_{1}~\text{{{in}}}~t:(T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})\theta
.

Proof.

By the fundamental property (Theorem
7.40
), we know

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
.

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
and
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
.
By the definition of binary logical relations and binary term interpretation, we know
there exists
\sigma_{11}
,
\sigma_{12}
,
\text{W}_{1}
,
v_{11}
and
v_{12}
, such that

•

\sigma_{1}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{11}\mid v_{11}

•

\sigma_{2}\mid\gamma_{2}(t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{12}\mid v_{12}

•

({\text{W};\text{W}_{1}},{v_{11}},{v_{12}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{T_{1}}}}]\!]^{\gamma}

•

({\sigma_{11}},{\sigma_{12}}):{\text{W};\text{W}_{1}}

•

v_{11}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi\cap q_{1})}))}

•

v_{12}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi\cap q_{1})}))}

•

{\sigma_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})})}{\sigma_{11}}

•

{\sigma_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})})}{\sigma_{12}}

By the fundamental property (Theorem
7.40
) again, we know

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{2}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{2}:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}
.
By the binary term interpretation, we know
there exists
\sigma_{21}
,
\sigma_{22}
,
\text{W}_{2}
,
v_{21}
and
v_{22}
, such that

•

\sigma_{1}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{2})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{21}\mid v_{21}

•

\sigma_{2}\mid\gamma_{2}(t_{2})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{22}\mid v_{22}

•

({\text{W};\text{W}_{2}},{v_{21}},{v_{22}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{T_{2}}}}]\!]^{\gamma}

•

({\sigma_{21}},{\sigma_{22}}):{\text{W};\text{W}_{2}}

•

v_{21}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi\cap q_{2})}))}

•

v_{22}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi\cap q_{2})}))}

•

{\sigma_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})})}{\sigma_{21}}

•

{\sigma_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})})}{\sigma_{22}}

Let
\sigma_{1a}={\sigma_{1}}\!\downarrow\!{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*})})}
,
and
\sigma_{1b}={\sigma_{1}}\!\downarrow\!{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*})})}
, and

\sigma_{1c}={\sigma_{1}}\!\downarrow\!{(\operatorname{dom}(\sigma_{1})-\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*})})-\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*})}))}
.

By Lemma
7.24
, we know,

(a)
\sigma_{11}=\sigma_{1a}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{1b}\ast\sigma_{1c}\ast\sigma_{fr11}
, for some
\sigma_{1a}^{\prime}
, where
\sigma_{fr11}\ast\sigma_{1}
; and

(b)
\sigma_{21}=\sigma_{1a}\ast\sigma_{1b}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{1c}\ast\sigma_{fr21}
, for some
\sigma_{1b}^{\prime}
, where
\sigma_{fr21}\ast\sigma_{1}
; and

(c)
\sigma_{fr11}\ast\sigma_{fr21}
.

Let
\sigma_{2a}={\sigma_{2}}\!\downarrow\!{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*})})}
,
and
\sigma_{2b}={\sigma_{2}}\!\downarrow\!{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*})})}
,
and

\sigma_{2c}={\sigma_{2}}\!\downarrow\!{(\operatorname{dom}(\sigma_{2})-\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}\mathord{*})})-\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathord{*})}))}
.

By Lemma
7.24
, we know,

(d)
\sigma_{12}=\sigma_{2a}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{2b}\ast\sigma_{2c}\ast\sigma_{fr12}
, for some
\sigma_{2a}^{\prime}
, where
\sigma_{fr12}\ast\sigma_{2}
; and

(e)
\sigma_{22}=\sigma_{2a}\ast\sigma_{2b}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{2c}\ast\sigma_{fr22}
, for some
\sigma_{2b}^{\prime}
, where
\sigma_{fr22}\ast\sigma_{2}
; and

(f)
\sigma_{fr12}\ast\sigma_{fr22}
.

From the left, by the deterministic of reduction semantics and (a), we know:

•

\sigma_{1}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{1a}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{1b}\ast\sigma_{1c}\ast\sigma_{fr11}\mid v_{11}

•

\sigma_{1a}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{1b}\ast\sigma_{1c}\ast\sigma_{fr11}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{2})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{1a}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{1b}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{1c}\ast\sigma_{fr11}\ast\sigma_{fr21}^{\prime}\mid v_{21}

From the right, by the deterministic of reduction semantics and (e), we know

•

\sigma_{2}\mid\gamma_{2}(t_{2})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{2a}\ast\sigma_{2b}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{2c}\ast\sigma_{fr22}\mid v_{22}

•

\sigma_{2a}\ast\sigma_{2b}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{2c}\ast\sigma_{fr22}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{2a}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{2b}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{2c}\ast\sigma_{fr{22}}\ast\sigma_{fr12}^{\prime}\mid v_{12}

Let
\sigma_{c}=\sigma_{1a}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{1b}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{1c}\ast\sigma_{fr11}\ast\sigma_{fr21}^{\prime}
and
\sigma_{d}=\sigma_{2a}\ast\sigma_{2b}^{\prime}\ast\sigma_{2c}\ast\sigma_{fr{22}}\ast\sigma_{fr12}^{\prime}
.

We know that there exists
\text{W}^{\prime}
, such that
({\sigma_{c}},{\sigma_{d}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}}
, and
\text{W};\text{W}_{1}\subseteq\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}
. and
\text{W};\text{W}_{2}\subseteq\text{W};W^{\prime}
.

By Lemma
7.7
, we know that
({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},{v_{11}},{v_{12}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{T_{1}}}}]\!]^{\gamma}
, and

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},{v_{21}},{v_{22}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{T_{2}}}}]\!]^{\gamma}

By the reduction semantics, and binary term interpretation, we know that

As
\gamma_{2}(t_{1})[y\mapsto v_{22}][x\mapsto v_{12}]=\gamma_{2}(t_{1})[x\mapsto v_{12}][y\mapsto v_{22}]
,
we have

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},{\gamma_{1}(t_{1})[x\mapsto v_{11}][y\mapsto v_{21}]},{\gamma_{2}(t_{1})[y\mapsto v_{22}][x\mapsto v_{12}]})\in\mathcal{E}[\![{T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}]\!]^{\gamma}_{{\varphi}}
by the fundamental propety (Theorem
7.40
) on the second assumption.

∎

Lemma 7.46 (
\lambda
-hoist).

If

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Gamma\ ,\ x:T^{\,p},y:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}\mathord{*}\cap(\varphi,x)\mathord{*}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,q,x,y}\,\vdash\,\ t:U^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
,
and
\theta=[q_{1}/y]
,
and
x\not\in\operatorname{fv}(U)
,
and
y\not\in\operatorname{fv}(U)
,
then

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models(\lambda x:T^{\,p}.\ \text{{{let}}}\ y=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ t)\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}(\text{{{let}}}\ y=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ \lambda x:T^{\,p}.t):(x:T^{\,p}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}U^{\,r}\theta)^{q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}
.

Proof.

t_{\lambda}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm def}}}{{=}}(\lambda x.\ \text{{{let}}}\ y=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ t)
, and
t_{b}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm def}}}{{=}}\text{{{let}}}\ y=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ t
.

By the fundamental property (Theorem
7.40
), we know
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{\lambda}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{\lambda}:(x:T^{\,p}\to^{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}}U^{\,r}\theta)^{q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}
.

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
and
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
.

By the definition of binary logical relations and function type interpretation, we know

•

\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(t_{\lambda})})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{1}(\text{W})

•

\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(t_{\lambda})})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}_{2}(\text{W})

Let

,

,

,

and

be arbitrary, such that

and we know that there exists

\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\mid\gamma_{2}(t_{b})[x\mapsto v_{2}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}\mid v_{4}

(c)

({\sigma^{\prime\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}):{\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}}

(d)

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime};\text{W}^{\prime\prime}},{v_{3}},{v_{4}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{U}}]\!]^{\gamma}

By Theorem
7.40
again, we know
\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}
.
By the definition of binary logical relations and term interpretation, we know
({\text{W}},{t_{1}},{t_{1}})\in\mathcal{E}[\![{T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varnothing}}}]\!]^{\gamma}_{{\varphi}}
.

,
\sigma_{b}
,

and

•

\sigma_{2}\mid\gamma_{2}(t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{b}\mid vx_{b}

•

\ast

\ast\ast

7.20

,

, and

.
By Lemma

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},{vx_{a}},{vx_{b}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{T_{1}}}]\!]^{\gamma}
.

Now, we can further specify the reduction of
t_{b}
as

(A)

\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\mid\gamma_{1}(t)[y\mapsto vx_{a}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mid v_{3}

(B)

\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\mid\gamma_{2}(t)[y\mapsto vx_{b}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}\mid v_{4}

Combining (a) and (A), and we have

(1)

\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\mid\gamma_{1}(t)[x\mapsto v_{1}][y\mapsto vx_{a}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mid v_{3}

(2)

\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\mid\gamma_{2}(t)[x\mapsto v_{2}][y\mapsto vx_{b}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}\mid v_{4}

By the second assumption and Theorem
7.40
, we know

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Gamma\ ,\ x:T^{\,p},y:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}\mathord{*}\cap(\varphi,x)\mathord{*}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,q,x,y}\models\ t\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t:U^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}

7.11

(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,q,x,y}}}]\!]
.
By
\ast
and
\ast\ast
, we apply Lemma
7.13
, and have

(\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime},\gamma;(x\mapsto(v_{1},v_{2}));(y\mapsto(vx_{a},vx_{b})))\in G[\![{{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Gamma\ ,\ x:T^{\,p},y:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}\mathord{*}\cap(\varphi,x)\mathord{*}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,q,x,y}}}]\!]

By the deterministic of the reduction semantics, we know
v_{3}=v_{5}
and

By the fact we have, the proof is done.

), we know

Lemma 7.49.

If

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Gamma\ ,\ x:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}\mathord{*}\cap\varphi\mathord{*}},y:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}\mathord{*}\cap(\varphi,x)\mathord{*}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi,x,y}\,\vdash\,\ t:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}

Lemma 7.50 (e-cse).

If

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\,\vdash\,t_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Gamma\ ,\ x:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}\mathord{*}\cap\varphi\mathord{*}},y:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}\mathord{*}\cap(\varphi,x)\mathord{*}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi,x,y}\,\vdash\,\ t:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}
,
and
\omega\notin{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
,
and
\theta=[x/y]
,
then

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models(\text{{{let}}}\ x=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ \text{{{let}}}\ y=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ t)\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}(\text{{{let}}}\ x=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ t\ \theta):(T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})\theta\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}}
.

Proof.

By the fundamental property (Theorem
7.40
), we know

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models t_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t_{1}:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
.

Let
(\text{W},\gamma)\in G[\![{{\Gamma^{\,\varphi}}}]\!]
and
({\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}):{\text{W}}
.
By the definition of binary logical relations and binary term interpretation, we know
there exists
\sigma_{11}
,
\sigma_{12}
,
\text{W}_{1}
,
v_{11}
and
v_{12}
, such that

•

\sigma_{1}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{11}\mid v_{11}

•

\sigma_{2}\mid\gamma_{2}(t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{12}\mid v_{12}

•

({\text{W};\text{W}^{\prime}},{v_{11}},{v_{12}})\in\mathcal{V}[\![{{\mathsf{T_{1}}}}]\!]^{\gamma}

•

({\sigma_{11}},{\sigma_{12}}):{\text{W};\text{W}_{1}}

•

v_{11}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi)})\cap\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(q_{1})}))}

•

v_{12}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi)})\cap\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(q_{1})}))}

•

{\sigma_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})})}{\sigma_{11}}

•

{\sigma_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})})}{\sigma_{12}}

As
\omega\not\in{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}
, we know
\text{W}_{1}=\varnothing
.

From the left, by the reduction semantics, we know there exists
\sigma_{11}
,
\sigma_{12}
,
\text{W}_{1}
,
v_{11}
and
v_{12}
, such that

•

\sigma_{1}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{11}\mid v_{11}

•

\sigma_{11}\mid\gamma_{1}(t_{1})\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{11}^{\prime}\mid v_{11}^{\prime}

•

\sigma_{11}^{\prime}\mid\gamma_{1}(t[x\mapsto v_{11}][y\mapsto v_{11}^{\prime}])\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\mid v_{1}

where
v_{11}=v_{11}^{\prime}
and
\sigma_{11}=\sigma_{11}^{\prime}
, by the deterministic of the reduction semantics.

Thus, the last reduction step can be re-written as

\sigma_{11}\mid\gamma_{1}(t[x\mapsto v_{11}][y\mapsto v_{11}])\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\mid v_{1},

where
\operatorname{dom}(\sigma_{11})=\operatorname{dom}_{1}{W}
.

By the second assumption and Theorem
7.40
, we know

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}[}\Gamma\ ,\ x:T_{1}^{\,q_{1}\mathord{*}\cap\varphi\mathord{*}},y:T_{2}^{\,q_{2}\mathord{*}\cap(\varphi,x)\mathord{*}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}]}^{\,\varphi,x,y}\models\ t\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}t:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}

.

By Lemma
7.49
, we know

\Gamma^{\,\varphi}\models(\text{{{let}}}\ x=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ t\ \theta)\mathrel{\approx_{\text{log}}}(\text{{{let}}}\ x=t_{1}\ \text{{{in}}}\ t\ \theta):(T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})\theta\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}}.

By definition of binary logic relations and term interpretation, after reducing
t_{1}
, we know there exists
\sigma_{1a}
,
\sigma_{2a}
,
\text{W}_{2}
,
v_{3}
and
v_{4}
, such that

•

\sigma_{11}\mid(\gamma_{1})(t\theta)[x\mapsto v_{11}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{1a}\mid v_{3}

•

\sigma_{12}\mid(\gamma_{2})(t\theta)[x\mapsto v_{12}]\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{{\ast}}_{\mathbf{v}}}\sigma_{2a}\mid v_{4}

•

({\sigma_{1a}},{\sigma_{2a}}):{\text{W};\text{W}_{1};\text{W}_{2}}

•

v_{11}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{1a}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(\varphi)})\cap\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}(q\ \theta)}))}

•

v_{12}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\scalebox{0.7}{${\sigma_{2a}}$}}{(\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(\varphi)})\cap\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}(q\ \theta)}))}

•

{\sigma_{1a}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{1}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\theta}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})})}{\sigma_{11}}

•

{\sigma_{2a}}\hookrightarrow^{\text{locs}({\gamma_{2}({\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\theta}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})})}{\sigma_{12}}

We know

(\gamma_{1}(t[x/y]))[x\mapsto v_{11}]=(\gamma_{1}(t[v_{11}/y]))[x\mapsto v_{11}]
.
Then we know
v_{1}=v_{3}
.
By the fact we have, the proof is done.

∎

8.
Optimization Rules and Equational Theory of
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{189617}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{132731}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{94808}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{G}$}}}}}}

In this section, we justify the soundness of the optimization rules shown in the main paper,
i.e.
,
they equate contextually equivalent graphs. Our approach reuses the logical relation
development for the direct-style
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
system from
Section

7
, through the use of
a “round-trip” translation exploiting the functional properties of dependency erasure and
synthesis. The key point is that this is already enough: dependencies, a type-level artifact derived
from the effects annotated in graphs, possess no operational meaning other than adhering to the order of
runtime effects, a consequence of type soundness.

8.1.
Logical and Contextual Equivalence for the Monadic Normal Form
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}

As the first step, we establish that we can restrict the logical relations development for the
direct-style
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
(
Section

7
) to
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
in MNF due to the results proved in

Section

4
. That is, MNF is a proper sublanguage of
\lambda^{*}_{\varepsilon}
and preserved by reductions
(
Lemmas

4.1
and
4.3
). Therefore, MNF
is also preserved by the logical equivalence (
Definition

7.3
), and contextual
equivalence (
Definition

7.1
). Note that by restricting to monadic syntax, the contexts

C
in
Figure

12
can only be of the shape

C::=\square\mid\text{{{let}}}~{x=C}~\text{{{in}}}~g\mid\text{{{let}}}~{x=\lambda y.C}~\text{{{in}}}~g\mid\text{{{let}}}~{x=b}~\text{{{in}}}~C

for which we can derive the specialized context typing rules (
Figure

16
).
Finally, some equational rules on expressions (
e.g.
, (
\beta
-inlining
),

Figure

15
) require a translation into MNF first to fit into the syntactic constraints
of
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
. This is always feasible due to the totality of the translation and its type-and-effect
preservation (
Lemma

4.2
).

Thus, without further ado, we will treat the development of
Section

7
as being defined over
\lambda^{*}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{248874}{197518}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{174211}{138262}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{124437}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}
.

Context for Contextual Equivalence

C::=\square\mid\text{{{let}}}~{x=C}~\text{{{in}}}~g\mid\text{{{let}}}~{x=\lambda y.C}~\text{{{in}}}~g\mid\text{{{let}}}~{x=b}~\text{{{in}}}~C

Context Typing Rules

C:(\Gamma^{\,\varphi};T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})\Rrightarrow(\Gamma^{\,\varphi};T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon}})

\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\ \Gamma\,\vdash\,S^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}}<:T^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\end{array}}{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\square:(\Gamma^{\,\varphi};{S}^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})\Rrightarrow(\Gamma^{\,\varphi};{T}^{\,q}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})\end{array}}

(c-hole)

\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}C:(\Gamma^{\prime\,\varphi^{\prime}};{U^{\prime}}^{\,r^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})\Rrightarrow(\Gamma^{\,\varphi};{U}^{\,r}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}})\hskip 9.24994pt(\Gamma,\,x:{U}^{\,r\mathord{*}\cap\varphi\mathord{*}})^{\varphi,x}\,\vdash_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{223985}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{223985}{177765}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{156789}{124436}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{111992}{88883}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{M}$}}}}}}\,g:{T}^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}}\\
x\notin\operatorname{fv}({T})\hskip 9.24994pt\theta=[r/x]\end{array}}{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\text{{{let}}}~x=C~\text{{{in}}}~g:(\Gamma^{\prime\,\varphi^{\prime}};{U^{\prime}}^{\,r^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{1}}})\Rrightarrow(\Gamma^{\,\varphi};({T}^{\,p}\ {\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{2}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\rhd}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6602,0.1484,0.3086}\bm{\varepsilon_{3}}})\theta)\end{array}}
